> **Implements Pillar**: Dread Before Chaos (P2 — the body's steadiness is what lets the two rhythms be read off the world instead of the controls); The Web Watches (P3 — every step, light and tool use is a stimulus the player owns); The World Never Lies (P1 — the body and its beam are never-shift anchors)

## Summary

The Player Controller & Input is the crewmate's body and hands: it turns keyboard, mouse or gamepad input into the two things the simulation accepts — intents (where I am walking, where my chest lamp points, whether I am running) and commands (cut, weld, pick up, drop, throw a glowstick) — and turns the simulation's answer back into a stiff, heavy, honest body on screen. The body is the same in the quiet boarding rhythm and the frantic breach: no adrenaline, no speed change, nothing that lets a player read the danger off their controls instead of off the world. Movement is whole-pixel integer kinematics with no physics body; the lamp is chest-mounted and hands-free; every acknowledgement is immediate and never encodes success; the sprite, its tools and its beam are anchors a hallucination can never edit. Sixteen systems depend on the intent/command split and the facing, reach and noise rules it fixes.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Core` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera` (both designed); position ownership adopted with `Ship Body & Interior Physics` (PC-14; technical-director ratification pending) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

## Overview

The Player Controller & Input is the crewmate's body and hands: it turns what the player does at the keyboard, mouse or gamepad into the two kinds of thing the simulation accepts — **intents** (where I am trying to walk, where I am pointing the beam, whether I am running) and **commands** (cut this hatch, toggle the beam, pick that up, drop it, start welding) — and it turns the simulation's answer back into a body on screen: five drawn facings and their mirrors, a stiff-weighted walk and run, a module carried at chest height, a stagger when the air goes bad, the lamp beam from the suit's chest-mounted light at the per-facing chest point. In the simulation it owns the player's intent and facing as a `PER_PLAYER` state block keyed by seat; whether it also owns ship-local position, or Ship Body & Interior Physics does, is the one open boundary it shares with that system (kernel Open Question 3) and is settled in this GDD provisionally.

The player operates this system every second of play, and its whole design goal is that they stop noticing it. It is active in the deliberate boarding rhythm (every step a decision, the beam swept slowly onto a doorway) and in the frantic breach rhythm (running a corridor while the ship fails around them) — and it is **the same body in both**: no adrenaline, no speed buff, no change of handling. The breach is frantic because of what moves in the world, not because the body changes (art bible 2.8).

It exists because the pillars put the player's body on the truth side of the game. *The World Never Lies*: the character sprite, its tools and its beam are never-shift anchors — a crewmate rendered as a horror still carries their true beam, and the body's honest states (rime, fogged visor, stagger) are a cross-check a low-sanity player can trust. *The Web Watches*: every step, every lit beam and every running stride is a stimulus the player owns — running is loud, and the controller is where that cost is paid. *Dread Before Chaos*: a slow, heavy body makes a door a decision. Every downstream system that needs a player to act — Interaction, Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Attention, the camera's follow target, the HUD's hand rail, co-op bodies as mirrored state — depends on the intent/command split this GDD fixes.

**Design-level only.** `CharacterBody2D` vs hand-rolled kinematics on a moving ship, how input actions are read, the animation driver — implementation choices → **becomes an ADR** (ship body / interior physics; input adapter). *(Framing: both the data layer and its player impact — `Core` category says technical, but this is the system the player is; fantasy direct.)*

## Player Fantasy

*Direct — the player operates this system every second.*

**The ship changes tempo; the body does not.** The player is the Sealed Technician: a round helmet on a square pack, stiff, weighted, upright, competent and fragile. The body moves the same way in a silent derelict and in a venting corridor. It walks without bounce, the pack rises and falls because the suit is sealed, and the chest-mounted lamp swivels on its plate independently of the torso: the body snaps between eight facings while the beam sweeps freely through 360°, so the beam follows the player's hand, not the player's shoulders. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier line "the beam turns exactly as fast as the player turns their body" was false by construction. The weight the pillar needs is carried by the stiff cycle, the run ramp, the carry slowdown and an optional walk-start ramp (`WALK_START_TICKS`, D.4), and is judged by the feel protocol in Game Feel; a non-lit swivel cue on the lamp housing is an art ask, Visual/Audio.)* Looking at something means lighting it. The body answers the frame the player presses and the ship confirms a tick later, so the hand never waits for the simulation. In the boarding rhythm that steadiness is patience: a deliberate step, a slow sweep, every door a decision. In the breach rhythm the same steadiness is the one thing the player can still trust. The panic is in the world — red sweeping, loops venting, a trail outrunning you down the corridor — and never in the controls. The player's only levers are their own choices: whether to run, which leans the body forward and is noise the web can hear, and where to point the light.

The anchor is one gesture made twice. The first time is early: the player steps out of the cryo pod, takes a few careful steps and swings the beam onto the doorway out, and the frame comes up out of the black. The second time comes later, with the same doorway or one like it and the alarm sweeping. The player swings the beam at the same speed, through the same arc, with the same hands. The room has changed and the body hasn't, which is how the player knows the danger is out there and not in them. When the body does change, the change is true. A stagger means the suit is failing, and the beam stays on the stumbling body: its origin rides the chest point, while its aim, shape and reach stay as the player and the light made them. Rime on the plates and a fogged visor are real cold and real bad air, never mood.

In co-op, a body tells the truth about its owner. A crewmate who walks like a technician and carries one of the four beam shapes is a crewmate, even through a hallucinated horror rig, because the rig is driven by their real motion and their real beam.

**Pillars served.** *Dread Before Chaos*: "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived… This pillar owns tempo and presentation." The body's steadiness keeps tempo in the world, where the tell can be read. *The Web Watches*: "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention… Restraint and stealth are real strategies." Running and aiming are the restraint choices the player holds every second. *The World Never Lies*: "Only instruments and perception can be wrong." The body is not an instrument, and its vital states are physical consequences. *Sanity Rewrites Reality*, secondary test: "−10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value → the false gauge." Sanity lies through the world, never through the legs.

**Design tests.**
- If we are debating whether the body or the beam may do something the player's input did not do and the simulation did not cause (aim snap or assist, auto-vault, auto-step, a camera-led turn, a scripted walk, a contextual animation that moves the character or reorients the beam): it may not. Presenting what happened is permitted: render interpolation, the per-facing chest point (beam origin), quantising facing to 8 directions while the beam rotates 360°, and immediate local acknowledgement.
- The fantasy has failed if the same input, at the same suit vitals and the same carried load, produces different movement or beam motion in the boarding rhythm, in the breach rhythm or at low sanity. That would mean some movement or aim parameter reads Attention, breach state or sanity level. It is a Pillar 2 violation, because the player would be reading the rhythm off their controls instead of off the world — not a tuning issue.

*What this section does not claim*: the controller does not own what light and noise cost (Emitter State and Attention), when rime, fog or stagger appear (the game-designer, via Suit and vitals), the speeds, acceleration and turn rates that make "stiff" responsive (Detailed Design and Game Feel), or the input bindings (ux-designer). The only permitted "shake" of the beam is its *origin* moving with the body — the chest point rides the run lean and the stagger frames (Pandorum's chest lamp); aim angle, cone shape, reach and temperature never change with the body, so body motion stays separable from sanity's light-lies. Whether the stagger's hand-point displacement needs a reduced-motion variant is flagged for `design/accessibility-requirements.md`; per kernel CR-11, presentation easing never changes the sim. *(Alternatives considered: "Looking is spending" — true, but credits the controller with Emitter State's and Attention's fantasy; "The body that cannot lie" — true, but credits the controller with Suit's honesty and risks implying a sanity self-tell, which art bible 5.3 forbids.)*

**Trade-off accepted.** The body never "rises to the occasion": no adrenaline burst, no breach sprint. Barotrauma's tempo, but your own legs. If playtests read the body as sluggish, the fix is base responsiveness for both rhythms, never a breach-only modifier. *We'll know this was right if* playtesters describe breaches as "the ship went crazy," not "my character got weird," and a code review finds no movement or aim value whose inputs include Attention, rhythm state or sanity.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Specialists consulted: game-designer (primary), gameplay-programmer, ux-designer; creative-director for Section B. Disagreements resolved and logged 2026-10-26.*

*Specialists consulted: game-designer (primary), gameplay-programmer (feel, kinematics, presentation contract), ux-designer (bindings, focus model, comfort, rebinding), with systems-designer (Suit thresholds) and creative-director (pillar alignment) as reviewers. Decisions logged 2026-10-26 (autonomous mode). Items tagged **→ ADR** are engine-integration choices deliberately left out of this behaviour spec. Rules are numbered `PC-n` (the kernel owns `CR-n`, the pipeline `C-n`/`PR-n`).*

**Vocabulary.** *Input Adapter*: the presentation-side object that reads devices, quantises, and **submits** commands as the local seat — kernel subscriber class `INPUT_SURFACE` (CR-10). *Controller Sim*: the simulation system that **owns** block `controller.state` (per controlled actor: move intent, gait, committed aim, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references; the chest point and hand point are **not** stored state but read-time `ControllerView` getters, `get_chest_x/_y` and `get_hand_x/_y`, ADR-0019 § 2.2, CT-28) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)* and runs step hook `controller.step` at divisor 1. *Avatar Presenter*: the `PRESENTATION`-class node that draws any crewmate body (local or remote) from read views; never submits. *LocomotionStep*: Body's pure integer kinematic step (PC-3). *Facing*: one of 8 octants (S, SE, E, NE, N, NW, W, SW), five drawn, three mirrored (art bible 5.6). *Raw aim*: the Adapter's local, un-quantised, per-frame aim; *committed aim*: `controller.state.aim_angle` as last applied. *Aim-live*: the aim source produced input this frame or (mouse) exists at all; *aim-idle*: a released stick. *Gait* ∈ {WALK, RUN}. *Body frame*: the ship-local frame of the body the actor is on (pipeline C2). *Channel*: a multi-tick action (cut, weld, patch) whose progress accrues in its owner's block. *Context*: the Adapter's input context ∈ {`gameplay`, `chart_table`, `inspect_card`, `chat`}.

**PC-1. Three parts, and the control-manifest lines that separate them.** (a) The **Input Adapter** reads devices, quantises, and submits `PLAYER`-origin commands as peer 1 acting on the entity the binding table binds to PlayerId 1 (CR-7). It writes no block, ever; it owns only local state (raw aim, last-acknowledged intents, hold timers, run latch, active device, context, pre-play tokens, menu latch and last NAV direction). It samples input for gameplay intents **in its own `_process`, at a process priority below the TickDriver node's, so before the driver's boundary service and tick loop** (ADR-0001 § 3; the MenuInput stream of PC-2a is sampled on render frames) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0001 GDD sync)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-15)*, so an intent submitted in frame N is frozen into tick T's P1 batch, applied in P2 and integrated by Body in P3 of the same tick — one tick of latency, never two. It never requests a Sanity, Attention or `PERCEPTION_OUT` read view: input mapping, dead-zones and aim source are HR-6 anchors and must be structurally unable to lie. (b) The **Controller Sim** registers `controller.state` (`SIM`, `persist = true`, `persist_scope = WORLD`, `scope = PER_PLAYER` keyed by PlayerId, `repl_hint = SHARED_SUMMARY`), command types `set_move_intent` and `set_aim`, the `PLAYER`-origin command **`give_up {actor}`** (`coalesce = NONE`, `max_age_ticks = 120`; validate: `loco_state == DOWNED` and no other bound seat that could revive (solo, MVP), else rejected `NOT_DOWNED`; apply: `death_tick := min(death_tick, T)`, so the existing D.14 check order does the rest in the same tick's `controller.step`: cap first, then death; PC-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D7, the solo "give up and reload" action)*, and hook `controller.step` (divisor 1, `after: suit.vitals`). It writes its block in its own `apply` handlers, inside its hooks, and through four published status-returning mutators (Class A in ADR-0003's taxonomy: each has a `check_*` twin and returns OK, REFUSED or -fault_code; ADR-0019 § 2.5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0019 sync)* that other owners' `apply` handlers and hooks call for cross-block writes (CR-9 a): `set_channel_ref(actor, owner_system, channel_id, face_octant)`, `clear_channel_ref(actor)`, `set_carry_ref(actor, item_id)`, `clear_carry_ref(actor)` (Interaction IA-36; Modular Ship Assembly calls `set_channel_ref(actor, ASSEMBLY, channel_id, face_octant)` and `clear_carry_ref(actor)`; `face_octant` is the owner's `octant_of(target anchor − foot)` computed at the `begin_*` apply and stored as `controller.state.channel_face`, so the facing lock of PC-4 rule 2 reads stored data that a MIRROR client has and never recomputes) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `face_octant` added; the mutators are not kernel-invoked callables, they take the shared `WriteScope`, report with `scope.fault` and return a status that callers propagate, while every Controller callable the kernel invokes — `validate`, `apply`, `controller.step`, `controller.noise`, `snapshot`, `restore`, `post_restore`, `read_view` — receives `ctx` and ends in `return ctx.complete()`, kernel CR-19, delegated ruling R3)*; it may not submit `PLAYER`-origin commands (CR-3). `face_octant` ∈ {0..7, NONE}, NONE being 8, the same sentinel as `octant`. The owner passes NONE (1) for object class `ACTOR_SELF` (the self-patch has no target anchor) and (2) when `anchor_o − foot` = (0, 0); `anchor_o` is the target subject's interaction anchor (D.9) and `foot` is the actor's `pos_curr`, both in the actor's body frame, the same pair `in_reach` uses, and the octant is `octant_of` (D.8). A value outside 0..8 is `scope.fault` with a non-OK status and writes nothing. Callers: Interaction's `begin_*` applies (IA-14) and Assembly's `begin_weld` / `begin_seam_cut` applies (MSA-30). The four mutators are published here and the siblings conform. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, blocker PC-1(b))* It also registers the `SYSTEM`-origin command type **`stagger {actor}`** (`allowed_origins {SYSTEM}`, `coalesce = NONE`, `max_age_ticks = 120`, no player binding; rejected `NOT_STAGGERABLE` in `DOWNED`, `DEAD` or `IN_POD`; `Applied` in `STAGGERED` without extending the lock, so a stagger never chains into a stun-lock), which Suit & Equipment submits on a two-band pressure drop within `STAGGER_WINDOW_TICKS` (SE-35) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)* (Horror Entities submits none, HE-6; PC-9), and a second divisor-1 hook **`controller.noise`** (`after: [body.integrate]`, D.13). Its `SIM` read view publishes `octant`, `run`, `gait_effective`, `aim_angle`, `aim_live`, `facing`, `loco_state`, `carry_ref`, `salvage_carry` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)*, `channel_ref`; the chest point and the hand point are not published fields but read-time getters on the same view (`get_chest_x/_y`, `get_hand_x/_y`, absolute body-frame ints from `BodyView.pos_x/_y` plus the frozen `CHEST_POINT` / `HAND_POINT` table, mirrored by facing; ADR-0019 § 2.6, CT-28) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)*; `loco_state` is the name Attention (AT-31: the living-player and `IN_POD` tests) and Horror (HE-33, C-HE-18) read. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system, modular-ship-assembly, suit-and-equipment, ship-body-and-interior-physics, attention-system, horror-entities)* (c) The **Avatar Presenter** reads `body.state` (integer position prev/curr, odometer), `controller.state` (facing, gait, locomotion state) and the `ControllerView` chest and hand getters (`get_chest_x/_y`, `get_hand_x/_y`, read-time, for the apex and offset terms only) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)*, `emitter.state` (beam lit, shape) and `tick_alpha`; for the *local* avatar only it also reads the Adapter's raw aim. It writes exactly: frame index, `flip_h`, integer position, overlay visibility, light position and light rotation — **never scale, rotation, skew or modulate on the body** (lint). *Rationale.* "Controller submits via `INPUT_SURFACE` only; never writes blocks" is true of the Adapter and false of the Controller Sim; one name for both would read as "this system has no block", and facing and gait would end up as presentation state that co-op cannot replicate and a save cannot restore. *Alternative rejected:* folding the Controller Sim into Ship Body — intent and facing are seat semantics (what the player asked for, read by Interaction, Attention and the HUD); position is a kinematic fact. Keeping them apart keeps the input record separate from the integrator.

**PC-2. Input model: contexts, actions, intents, commands.** The game reads **named actions** (`StringName`, rebindable), never raw keys and never `device == 0` (4.7: `InputEvent.DEVICE_ID_MOUSE` / `DEVICE_ID_KEYBOARD`; identify devices by event type). **Decided in ADR-0019 § 3.2 and § 3.3**: `InputMap` actions registered at runtime from data with a non-zero engine deadzone, the any-device wildcard and `physical_keycode`; ordered edges latched in `_input` (key, mouse-button and pad-button events only; pad-axis motion is continuous) and continuous state polled in `_process`; devices identified by event type *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0019 sync)*.

*Contexts.* Exactly one context is active: `gameplay`, `chart_table`, `inspect_card`, `chat`. Entering any non-`gameplay` context sends `set_move_intent {NONE}` and clears the run latch; entering `chart_table` or `chat` also submits `cancel_channel` for a hold- **or** toggle-mode channel (in the co-op overlay the sim keeps running and a channel the player cannot see must not keep burning); entering `inspect_card` does **not** cancel the channel, so the Pillar 4 "second instrument" check on the pipe being welded is free (the zeroed move intent is not a move, the channel continues, `interact` is still tracked for hold mode and a release inside the card still cancels; `cancel` closes the card first and a second `cancel` cancels the channel). **Movement closes the card** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D94, the Controller's context-matrix amendment adopted; the In-World Instruments fallback "buffer and replay the first move press" is void)*: a **fresh** movement input in `inspect_card` (a `move_*` key going down, or the left stick crossing `MOVE_DEADZONE` from below `MOVE_DEADZONE − MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT`) returns the context to `gameplay` **and** submits `set_move_intent` for that octant in the same frame, in that order, so the first press both closes the card and moves the avatar (nothing is swallowed, nothing is buffered). A direction already held when the card opened is latched and does not count until it is released (or the stick falls below the exit band) and pressed again, so opening the card while walking does not close it on the next frame. A move that closes the card while a channel is running is an ordinary non-`NONE` move intent and aborts the channel exactly as it does in `gameplay`. Any transition of `loco_state` to `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD` or `IN_POD` returns the context to `gameplay` on the same frame (the Adapter observes the published state; the card or overlay closes and the cursor is hidden again). Pause is reachable in every state including `DOWNED`: `chart_table` and `request_pause` are Adapter-level and never gated by `loco_state`. The full **context × action matrix** is in the table below. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; supersedes the earlier "cancel all channels on every non-`gameplay` entry" note for `inspect_card` only. Routed to Instruments and Interaction as a cross-GDD note.)* The OS cursor is **hidden and confined** in `gameplay` (HUD HS-52: no cursor in `gameplay`; the beam is the pointer) and **shown and released** in `chart_table` and `chat` (in `chart_table` its shape follows the layer's `cursor_hint`, PC-2a (g)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-15)*; in `inspect_card` it stays **hidden and inert** (HUD HS-52: `set_aim` stays live there, and a visible cursor would be a second pointer over the beam; PC-16). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer, in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view; cursor visibility in `inspect_card` corrected 2026-10-27 — design review, HS-52 owns it)* Zoom is reachable from `gameplay` and `inspect_card`, and pause from `gameplay` (from `inspect_card` the first `chart_table` press closes the card, see the context return table) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `chat` (VS-B) swallows text keys and is the only keyboard-only flow in the core loop (HR-14).

*Default bindings (`gameplay` context).*

| Action | KB+M | Gamepad | Kind | What the Adapter submits |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `move_up/down/left/right` | WASD | left stick | continuous | part of `set_move_intent` |
| `run` | Shift | LT | hold (toggle option, PC-3) | part of `set_move_intent` (`run`) |
| aim | mouse | right stick | continuous | `set_aim`; raw aim kept locally |
| `beam_toggle` | F | Y | press | `toggle_beam` (owner: Emitter State) |
| `interact` | E (alias LMB) | A | press / hold | the command type **Interaction declares** for the focused object (PC-6) |
| `cancel` | X | B | press | `cancel_channel` if channelling; else closes the current UI |
| `carry_drop` | G | X | press | `drop` (owner: Interaction) |
| `tool_1..6`, `tool_cycle` | 1–6, Tab | d-pad right / left (next / previous tool) | press | `equip_tool` (owner: Suit & Equipment) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pad cycle moved from RB / LB to the d-pad so RB can carry `throw`; ux-designer ruling adopted)* |
| `aim_up/down/left/right` | arrow keys | — | continuous | keyboard aim: the four keys compose an 8-way vector exactly like movement (D.6 composition, opposite keys cancel); `aim_q` = the octant centre `512 × o`, `live = true` while any is held; `set_aim` as in D.7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: makes keyboard-only aim possible; proposed, ux-designer owns the keys)* |
| `aim_hold` | Left Ctrl | — | hold | while held, the four `move_*` keys steer **aim** (as the arrow keys do) and `set_move_intent {NONE}` is sent: stand and look, hands on one block of keys *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; proposed, ux-designer owns)* |
| `zoom_in`, `zoom_out` | wheel up/down, = / − | d-pad up / down | press (edge) | no command — pipeline C6 consumes (PC-16) |
| `inspect` | R (alias RMB) | RT | press | context switch to `inspect_card`; no command — a presentation-only switch (IA-38), not a verb (HR-14 mirror card; ships at MVP, IW-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)* |
| `chart_table` | Esc | Start | press | `request_pause` solo (control plane via the menu); overlay only in co-op |
| `throw` | Q | RB | press | `throw_glowstick {aim_angle}` (owner: Interaction; charges: Suit & Equipment) — adopted 2026-10-26, amendment (9); *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pad binding moved from R3 to RB (R3 pressed the aim stick and collided with the dev chord); ux-designer ruling adopted)* |
| `give_up` | Backspace | View | hold-to-confirm (D.10; two-tap with `confirm_mode = TAP`) | `give_up {actor}`, **live only while `loco_state == DOWNED` in solo**; ignored in every other state and context. The solo "give up and reload" action: it ends the downed wait early and takes the same `DOWNED → DEAD` path as the timer, so the restart from the last autosave is unchanged. The visible confirm plate for this action is drawn by HUD (a DOWNED-only plate placed just outside the chest keep-out, HUD HS-42(b) and HS-39: a plate on the chest point would violate HUD's `PROMPT_KEEPOUT_PX` 16; UI Requirements 1); the Controller draws nothing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D7; re-review: HUD owns the plate)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| *(unbound)* | — | L3, R3, LB | — | reserved for the player's rebinds (dev builds only: L3 + R3 is the Debug Overlay reveal chord, PC-2 dev-only note; the chord no longer shadows any gameplay action) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: View now carries `give_up`)* |

The wheel is **zoom**, not tool cycling — the first conflict the per-context rebinding model catches (PC-13).

*Context × action matrix.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: new; the Adapter's policy per context, one row per action family. "ignore" = the press is dropped before submit with no pre-play.)*

| Action family | `gameplay` | `chart_table` | `inspect_card` | `chat` (VS-B) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `move_*`, `run`, `aim_*` keys | live | navigation only (D.6 four-way) | a **fresh** move past `MOVE_DEADZONE` **closes the card and submits `set_move_intent` in the same frame**; a direction held at card entry is latched until released; `run` and `aim_*` keys ignored; mouse/stick **aim stays live** (PC-16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D94)* | ignore |
| `give_up` | live only in `DOWNED` (solo); else ignore | ignore | ignore | ignore |
| mouse / right-stick aim (`set_aim`) | live | not sent | **live** | not sent |
| `interact` (begin, tap, hold-confirm) | live | accept (chart) | ignore as a new press; a held channel's `interact` is still tracked (hold mode) | ignore |
| channel already running on entry | continues | **cancelled** (`cancel_channel`, both modes) | **continues**; `cancel` closes the card first | **cancelled** |
| `cancel` | `cancel_channel` if channelling, else closes UI | back | closes the card (channel untouched) | closes chat |
| `beam_toggle`, `throw`, `carry_drop`, `tool_*` | live | ignore | ignore | ignore |
| `zoom_in/out`, `chart_table` (pause) | live | `chart_table` closes; zoom ignore | zoom live; `chart_table` **closes the card first** (context return table), channel untouched, no pause on that press *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | zoom ignore |
| `inspect` | opens the card | ignore | closes the card | ignore |
| forced return to `gameplay` | — | on `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD` | on the same four | on the same four |

*Context return table* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the context the Adapter returns to; `Esc` is `chart_table`):

| From | Action | To | Channel and side effects |
|---|---|---|---|
| `inspect_card` | `chart_table` (Esc) | `gameplay` — the card closes first; **no pause and no overlay opens on that press** | channel untouched; a second `chart_table` press, now in `gameplay`, opens the chart / requests pause |
| `inspect_card` | `cancel` or `inspect` | `gameplay` | channel untouched; a second `cancel` in `gameplay` submits `cancel_channel` |
| `inspect_card` | fresh move past `MOVE_DEADZONE` | `gameplay` and `set_move_intent` in the same frame | the move aborts a running channel |
| `chart_table` | `chart_table` or `cancel` | `gameplay` | a channel was cancelled on entry and is not resumed |
| `chat` | `cancel` | `gameplay` | as `chart_table` |
| any | forced return (`STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD`) | `gameplay` | the cursor hides |

So `chart_table` is never entered from `inspect_card`: Esc over the card can no longer cancel the channel the card was built to preserve.

*Default bindings (`chart_table` context).* No new action. The Adapter (never the Chart Table) reads the physical controls of `move_up/down/left/right` (WASD, left stick) plus the arrow keys and the d-pad, four-way under `MOVE_DEADZONE` / `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT`; accept is the physical controls of `interact` in this context minus every mouse button (E, gamepad A), plus Enter and Space as fixed additional keyboard accept bindings of this context; back is `cancel` (X / B); close is `chart_table` (Esc / Start); every mouse button and the wheel are `POINTER` events only, never an accept. Conflicts are checked per context (PC-13). The gameplay-context `interact` alias LMB (default bindings table) is unchanged. All of it reaches the layer as the MenuInput stream (PC-2a). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-15)* **Console seam:** at the pod console the Adapter injects the host-local `ChartPick` into `pod_hop`'s payload; a console press with no pick submits nothing and opens the chart (`chart_table` context) instead — a context switch, not a verb choice (Chart Table ASK-CT-1, ASK-CT-2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from chart-table-web-map-and-menus)*

**PC-2a. MenuInput stream (Adapter-owned; consumer: the Chart Table layer, CT-45).** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-15)* The Adapter publishes a presentation-local MenuInput event stream while the active context is `chart_table`; before a campaign is loaded (the boot-flow screens) the Adapter runs in the `chart_table` context with no gameplay sink and publishes the same stream. It is sampled on render frames (the gameplay-intent sampling of PC-1 (a) happens in the Adapter's `_process` just before the TickDriver and serves gameplay intents only); all events of one render frame are delivered in arrival order at the start of that frame, before the layer's update. **Events:** `NAV_DOWN{dir, source}` and `NAV_UP{dir}` (`dir` ∈ {UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT}; `source` ∈ {STICK, DPAD, ARROW, WASD}); `ACCEPT_DOWN`, `ACCEPT_UP`; `BACK`; `CLOSE`; `FOCUS_OUT`, `FOCUS_IN`; `DEVICE_CHANGED{device}`; `POINTER{kind ∈ {MOVE, DOWN, UP, WHEEL}, button, position}`, where `position` is the cursor in physical window px, forwarded unconverted (the root viewport mouse position; root stretch is disabled, ADR-0008 § 5); the Chart Table layer subtracts `RenderLayout.picture_rect.position` itself, and the Controller's aim path (PC-5) subtracts the world picture's origin exactly once, so each consumer subtracts once and the Adapter's stream value is never subtracted twice; the unit is settled, not **[UNV]** (AC-CT-135 (f) only confirms it) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*. **The Adapter owns:** (a) **echo suppression**: an OS key echo of a held E, Enter, Space, Esc or X produces no second event (probed 2026-10-29 on 4.7.2: a synthetic echo event does not re-fire `is_action_just_pressed`; real OS key repeat is still **[UNV]** until PC-INPUT-SPIKE-1 (d), a RECORDED item with a held-set fallback *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0019 sync)*); (b) **deadzone hysteresis**: a stick takes a NAV edge on crossing `MOVE_DEADZONE` from below `MOVE_DEADZONE − MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT` and releases below `MOVE_DEADZONE − MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT`, the same band as the `inspect_card` movement rule (PC-2 contexts); (c) **the cardinal-axis rule**: a stick yields one NAV direction at a time, the axis with the larger magnitude, a tie going to the vertical axis; the taken direction changes only after the stick has re-passed the exit band, so a diagonal drift cannot alternate; auto-repeat of a held NAV direction is the layer's (Chart Table D.9); (d) **latch-until-released**: for any physical control whose press closed the layer (Resume accept, `chart_table`, `cancel`) the Adapter emits nothing from that control, to the layer or to gameplay, until it is physically released and pressed again, including across the `PAUSED → RUNNING` resync (Edge Cases, kernel `PAUSED`); for a console press that opened the layer, the open press's release and repeats are latched the same way; (e) **focus**: `FOCUS_OUT` releases every held menu control and clears navigation state, and the layer's hold fills abort; on `FOCUS_IN` nothing is held and fresh presses are required; (f) **`DEVICE_CHANGED{device}`** is published when the last-used-device glyph set changes under PC-13 (immediately, except inside `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` of the previous change, when it is published at the window end), so the layer adds no delay; (g) **the cursor hint**: the layer publishes `cursor_hint` ∈ {DEFAULT, SELECTABLE}; the Adapter, its only `Input` caller, applies the pointer-cursor shape. The `zoom_*` edges are never emitted in this context (PC-16). The stream carries no gameplay command and no simulation read. Its code lives in the Adapter's registered files (AC-PCI-176).

*Dev-only actions (dev feature tag only).* The Adapter registers the ten actions of registry `debug_overlay_actions` — `debug_overlay_toggle`, `debug_overlay_page_next`, `debug_overlay_page_prev`, `debug_overlay_mode`, `debug_overlay_dump`, `debug_overlay_reveal`, `debug_overlay_mark`, `debug_overlay_cursor_next`, `debug_overlay_cursor_prev`, `debug_overlay_activate` (placeholder defaults toggle F3, page next F4, page prev Shift+F4, mode Insert, dump Home, reveal Ctrl+Shift+F3 with the pad chord L3 + R3, mark End, cursor next PageDown, cursor prev PageUp, activate Delete; F5-F8 avoided as editor run, pause and stop shortcuts; Debug Overlay DO-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* — **only under the dev feature tag**, and forwards them host-locally to the Overlay: never a command, never in the binding table Settings sees, never in a rebinding, preset or accessibility enumeration, absent from release. The Adapter is their only `Input` reader (Gaps: the cross-GDD lint collision). **Pad chord:** the dev chord L3 + R3 shadows no gameplay action now that `throw` is on RB; L3 and R3 are unbound by default in every build. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values; rewritten 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "R3 consumed by the chord before `throw`" rule is void)*

*Intents vs commands (CR-4).* Two types are **intents**, `coalesce = LATEST_PER_ACTOR_PER_TICK`, `max_age_ticks = 30`: **`set_move_intent {octant: 0..7 | 8 = NONE, run: bool}`** and **`set_aim {aim_angle: int, live: bool}`** in **1/4096-turn units** (`live` is `aim_live`: it travels with the angle because facing rules 4–6 branch on it in the sim). Both intents carry `actor_binding = OWNED_BY_ISSUER` with `actor_source = body.state` (the avatar's Body `actor_id`, kernel CR-7), like commands. Everything else is a **command**: `coalesce = NONE`, `max_age_ticks = 120`, `actor_binding = OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source` as its owner declares (`body.state` for every avatar-acting verb; `emitter.state` for `toggle_beam`, whose `actor` is the seat's beam id) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The Adapter sends `set_move_intent` **only on change** of octant or `run`, with the stick quantised to 8 octants under `MOVE_DEADZONE` = 0.25 and `OCTANT_HYSTERESIS_DEG` = 5°. In one frame it submits `set_move_intent` **before** any command (submission order is the P2 apply order — the kernel stamps `seq` internally per (peer, source) from it, and callers never choose it, delegated ruling R2 — so a stop and a `begin_*` in the same frame apply in that order, PC-7). It sends `set_aim` when the quantised value differs from `last_acked` by **≥ 1/128 turn (2.8°)**, **or** the aim source changed (mouse ↔ stick ↔ keys), **or** `live` changed, **or** a settle condition holds (inside the threshold but differing at all, for `AIM_SETTLE_TICKS` = 6) — **rate-limited to one per `AIM_SEND_INTERVAL` = 3 ticks**. At rest that is zero sends; a sweep is ≤ 20 sends/s, far inside `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK`. **No input buffering**: `ACTION_BUFFER_TICKS` = 0; a press during a channel is submitted and answered `BUSY`, honestly; nothing is queued for "after". *Rationale:* buffering hides the sim's answer behind a presentation guess; Zomboid's immediacy is the model. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Zero buffering is a recorded, deliberate trade, not an oversight:** an `interact` for a `CHANNEL` verb pressed one to three ticks before the move keys are up is rejected `MOVING` (PC-7) and costs one honest re-press; the same-tick case (release W and press E in one frame) is solved by ordering, not by buffering (intents are submitted first and validation reads `intent_octant`, PC-7). Adapter-side lever, **off at MVP**: `CHANNEL_BEGIN_GRACE_TICKS` (0–3, default **0**) would hold a `begin_*` press until the Adapter's own last-submitted octant is NONE for at most that many ticks; a playtest finding of "my weld never starts" is the trigger to raise it. *Ignored-input cues (accessibility):* four presses are dropped by Adapter-local knowledge alone and play one short dull cue (`cue_input_ignored`, the same id for all four, no visual, never read from a sim result): `toggle_beam` inside the rate-limit window (PC-5), `interact` with no focused object, `run` while `carry_ref ≠ none`, and `run` while the player's own inventory view shows a HEAVY item stowed (`stowed_heavy`, user ruling D18 and D.4; the name is the one Suit & Equipment publishes, registry `stowed_heavy`, and the earlier `heavy_stowed` is retired *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, general rule G1)*; it is the consequence of the player's own stow command, not a hidden reading) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D18)*. The cue is Adapter-local and so cannot reveal a sim answer; `run` under Suit's cap gets none (the cap is Suit's state, shown by the breath and warmth readings).

*Immediate acknowledgement and the pre-play token (CR-4).* On submit the Adapter pre-plays **body and sound only, never the effect**: the hand's switch animation, the tool click, the bar starting. Each pre-play is keyed by an opaque integer **`correlation`** the Adapter attaches to the command (a per-Adapter counter; the kernel echoes it unchanged in the result or rejection event; `submit()` stays void and fire-and-forget, and the kernel, not the caller, stamps `seq`) in a `correlation → pre-play token` map, so a late result cancels the right pre-play *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R2)*. The beam light is the one object that is not pre-played (PC-5). **Rejection classes** (declared by each verb's owner, Interaction's GDD): *immediate* reasons — `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `BUSY`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `ACTOR_NOT_OWNED`, `PAUSED`, every admission reason — **withdraw the ack at once** (the sound stops, the bar vanishes within one frame of the result); the free-lie-detector case of Pillar 4 (a device the player perceives as broken or fixed) is **not a rejection**: the command is `Applied` and the channel resolves to a `PRECONDITION` outcome (Interaction, KRN:96), so the ack is never withdrawn — the work loop and the bar continue until Interaction's channel state signals the outcome. **The ack frame for a command whose channel will complete and one whose channel will resolve to `PRECONDITION` must be pixel- and sound-identical.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system)* Rejection is never a shake (C9, HR-13).

*Non-audio counterpart (HUD-owned; reduced to the MOVING tell).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated CD ruling, HUD re-review K43)* Every negative cue was audio-only, so deaf and hard-of-hearing players got none, and nothing taught the three core rules (channels need stillness, prompts need light, destructive verbs need a hold). The only non-audio counterpart at MVP is the pre-press tell for a `begin_*` press that the actor's **own** state would block (`MOVING`): Interaction's `focus_view.press_blocked_moving`, drawn by HUD as a hollow keycap on `CHANNEL` and `HOLD_CONFIRM` plates (Interaction IA-41, AC-IA-142). The Adapter publishes **no** `input_ignored_view(seat)` and no reason class to the HUD: a visible response to a `NOT_LIT` (or any subject-dependent) press would tell the player something is at the unlit subject, the unlit-subject oracle Pillar 3 forbids, so the glyph dim on `NOT_LIT`, `BUSY`-by-other-seat, `OUT_OF_RANGE` and `INVALID_TARGET` presses is refused; the HUD still holds the restored keycap for `BAR_REJECT_HOLD_TICKS` on a plate Interaction has already shown (HUD HS-41). The other ignored-input cues (`cue_input_ignored`: `toggle_beam` in its window, `interact` with no focus, `run` while carrying) stay audio-only at MVP; a non-audio counterpart for them is deferred to `design/accessibility-requirements.md` (Open Question 26) and must not key on subject existence or lighting. **First-use hints are refused**: no caption, no Settings "seen" flag, no "reset profile" interaction (HUD HS-57, anti-pillar 4; teaching belongs to the world, Cryo Bay onboarding). **Playtest metric:** ≥ 80 % of first-time testers complete the first weld within two attempts; a miss reopens the Cryo Bay onboarding (HUD OQ-HS-29), not the rules and not a HUD hint.

*Resync rule (one rule, five triggers).* The Adapter keeps `last_acked` per intent type and must **resend its current quantised value, bypassing the change filter once**, when: (i) an intent result arrives `Rejected(STALE)` (kernel Edge Cases: a stale "stop" otherwise leaves the actor moving); (ii) the kernel leaves `PAUSED` (CR-16 rejects intents immediately while paused, so a release during a pause was lost); (iii) `LOADING → RUNNING`; (iv) window focus returns, or the active gamepad reconnects; (v) **`TRANSFER`**: the actor's `body_of_actor` changes, i.e. a pod arrival (`IN_POD →` the locomotion-class member) or a section-detach body transfer, because the new body's first tick must act on the keys physically held *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pod arrival and body transfer were handled only as an ad-hoc "treated as trigger iii" in Edge Cases)*. For a hold-mode channel it compares the actual button state to the last submitted one on each of those and submits `cancel_channel` if the button is up. **Window focus loss** submits `set_move_intent {NONE}` and `cancel_channel` if channelling, and marks every held action released; **the loss of the active gamepad** (a disconnect, mid-weld included) is treated identically (the same zero intent and cancel, the held pad actions released, the keyboard and mouse stay live, and a reconnect resyncs under trigger iv), and **the click or key press that returns window focus is consumed by the Adapter** and never reaches a binding (an LMB alias of `interact` would otherwise fire on refocus) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Invariant (testable): the sim's intent equals the player's hands within one tick of the kernel being `RUNNING`.

**PC-3. Movement: ship-local, 8-way, two gaits, whole base pixels per tick, no physics body.** Movement is in the **body frame** (pipeline C2); the interior never shows ship motion. Input is **8-way**; gait is the `run` action, never stick deflection. *Rationale:* the noise cost of running (PC-10) must be a deliberate input, not a thumb slip (Sirlin's test for a real decision), and analog speed would be a continuum of non-integer px/tick, which PR-4 rule 3 forbids at camera quantum 1.

*Speeds — derivation.* The pipeline routes "whole base px per tick when the quantum is 1" (PR-4 rule 3) and asserts two build invariants on `v_max`: `LAG_CUT > v_max·τ_follow` → `v_max < 266 px/s`, **and** `0.30·L_h > v_max·τ_follow + L_MAX` at the default field → `0.18·v_max + 72 ≤ 108` → **`v_max < 200 px/s`**. The second binds (the gameplay-programmer's reading and the game-designer's coincide); the brief's 266 is not the operative bound. At `TICK_RATE` = 60 the whole-px/tick ladder is 60 / 120 / 180 / 240 px/s, so the integer ceiling is **3 px/tick = 180 px/s** (lag 32.4 px; 32.4 + 72 = 104.4 ≤ 108). The pipeline's placeholders 90 / 150 px/s are 1.5 / 2.5 px/tick — non-integer under its own routed rule — and are superseded (routed correction).

| Knob | Value | px/s at 60 Hz | tiles/s | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v_walk` (registry: `V_WALK`) | **2 px/tick** | 120 | 2.5 | Deliberate but not tedious: the 13-tile default field crosses in ≈ 5 s; the boarding rhythm's pace is set by doors and reading. 1 px/tick recorded as the playtest alternative |
| `v_run` (registry: `V_RUN`) | **3 px/tick** | 180 | 3.75 | The integer ceiling under the inner-box invariant; 1.5× walk reads with the forward lean (5.2) and never clamps the camera |
| `v_carry` (registry: `V_CARRY`) | **1 px/tick** | 60 | 1.25 | A module at chest height halves the walk; extraction under rising Attention is the sortie's tension, and the slowdown is *seen* (carry-walk cycle), never a hidden stat |
| `v_max` | = `v_run` | 180 | — | Read by the pipeline's G check |

All speeds are in px/tick and are invalidated by a `TICK_RATE` change (kernel Open Question 12).

*Kinematics (Body's mechanism, this GDD's contract — Body to confirm).* **The avatar has no physics body.** Ship Body runs a hand-rolled integer kinematic step `LocomotionStep.step(state, intent, collide: Callable)` — a pure `RefCounted`, headless-testable: `pos: Vector2i` (base px, body frame) plus `sub: Vector2i` in **1/256-px fixed point**; velocity from an 8-entry integer unit table (cardinal 256, **diagonal 181/256** per axis, so diagonal speed equals cardinal speed to 0.1 %); **axis-separated 1-px AABB sweeps**, x then y every tick, against Layout's integer footprint rectangles, consumed as `SIMULATION` data (walls 24 px deep under the up-screen convention, closed door leaves, props, machinery) — sliding along a wall falls out of axis separation; `sub` **resets to zero on stop**; the foot box is **16 × 8 base px**, horizontally centred on the foot origin, which is the box's top row (`[pos.x − 8, pos.x + 8) × [pos.y, pos.y + 8)`; registry `foot_box`, `FOOT_BOX_W`, `FOOT_BOX_H`). Consequence: the avatar's position is a **pure function of the command stream and the layout**, so the kernel's G3 replay needs no recorded physics observations for crewmates — the Controller/Body pair is deterministic by construction. The kernel's `snap_baseline` on entry to RUNNING baselines the Body's read view; Body sets no `previous := current` in `post_restore`, and preserves `sub`, which resets only on the first octant change (registry `move_octant_reset`, `body_state_block`) (pipeline C5; ADR-0008 § 6.1) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics, ship-section-and-layout-data-model)* *Why normalised diagonals:* an un-normalised run diagonal (3·√2 = 4.24 px/tick = 254 px/s) would exceed the camera's pan ceiling and cut on every diagonal sprint, and it is the classic diagonal-sprint exploit. *Alternatives rejected:* `CharacterBody2D` (non-deterministic under G3, and the moving composite ship makes the engine's moving-platform handling the risk the systems index names); integer diagonal pairs ((1,1) is 71 % of walk, (2,2) is 141 %).

*Acceleration, stopping, turning.* Start: `RUN_RAMP_TICKS` = 6 — a run begins at `v_walk` for 6 ticks, then `v_run` (integer-only; escape is never blocked because the body is already moving at walk speed on tick 1). Walk start is instant by default; `WALK_START_TICKS` (0–6, default **0**, D.4) is a weight lever that makes the first N ticks of a walk move at 1 px/tick, a rhythm-independent response to the "heavy, not sluggish" question *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; off until the feel protocol in Game Feel asks for it)*. Stop: `STOP_DECEL_TICKS` = 0 (range 0–3, integer steps only). Facing change: instant, mid-cycle (PC-4). Positions are integer base px at commit, so a stop is always on the pixel grid; **no tile snapping** on stop (an uncaused motion the player did not make; C-R's "never reverses unless the player did" extends to the body). No actor pushes another at MVP.

*Run latch (toggle option).* With run as a toggle, the latch is set by a press and **auto-clears** after `RUN_LATCH_CLEAR` = 30 ticks of `octant == NONE`, on channel start, and on entering any non-`gameplay` context — so a latched run never survives a stop the player did not re-decide.

**PC-4. Facing: five drawn, three mirrored; derived from committed aim when the aim is live.** Facing is recomputed **every tick from committed state, with no easing, mid-cycle**, using a **2-quanta sector hysteresis** (2 × 1/128 turn = 5.6°) at octant boundaries. Priority order:
1. **Staggered / downed / dead**: facing frozen.
2. **Channelling**: facing held at the stored `channel_face` for the whole channel (the work loop is two-handed and tool-forward, 5.2; `channel_face` is written once by `set_channel_ref`'s `face_octant` at the `begin_*` apply, so a MIRROR client reads it and never recomputes it); the beam still follows aim from the locked chest point. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* A channel whose `channel_face` is none (ACTOR_SELF) holds `facing_prev` for its whole length. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, blocker PC-1(b))*
3. **Running**: facing = movement octant regardless of aim. *Rationale:* the run cycle is a forward lean of the whole figure; a lean sideways to its motion reads as the horror's asymmetry (3.2 negation rule). The beam is engine light rotating 360° from the chest point (5.6), so a runner's light may point behind them — honest. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "and tense" claim was struck: retreating at full run with the beam on the pursuer cost only run noise, and the camera look-ahead even showed what is behind. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D6 option B, Open Question 19. **While running the camera look-ahead is weighted toward the movement direction** (the pipeline's D.7 `LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT`, cited by PC-5 "Two consumers" and D.7b; the Controller owns no second knob — *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, general rule G1*); the beam, `aim_angle`, `set_aim` and the sim are untouched, so turning your back on the pursuer costs the view of it, by presentation alone. The "and tense" claim is restored in this narrower form: the tension is the view, not a penalty. Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
4. **Aim-live** (mouse always; stick deflected): facing = aim octant, whether walking or stationary and **whether or not the beam is lit** — mouse players face the cursor and back-pedal. *Rationale (ux-designer, adopted):* the camera already leans into the aim regardless of beam state (pipeline C4); body and camera must agree, or a beam toggle would turn the body without turning the frame.
5. **Aim-idle, moving**: facing = movement octant.
6. **Aim-idle, stationary**: facing holds.

Back-pedal and side-step use the single walk cycle; when `dot(move, facing) < 0` the Presenter plays the walk cycle **reversed** (zero extra frames). Back-pedal speed is full `v_walk` (`BACKPEDAL_FACTOR` = 1.0; any non-integer value breaks whole-px cadence, so a penalty, if ever wanted, is "walk only" or an integer). Mirroring: SW, W, NW are the SE, E, NE sheets x-flipped (`flip_h`); the hand point's x is negated and the tool changes hands (accepted, 5.6); the chest point barely moves (`|x| ≤ 2`). *Alternative rejected:* the earlier draft's "facing from aim only while the beam is lit" — body and camera would disagree whenever the beam is off.

**PC-5. Aim and the beam.** *Sources.* Mouse: raw aim = direction from the **aim origin** `AIM_ORIGIN = floor(pos_curr) + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)` (a fixed reference chest height, independent of facing, sheet and animation row; default `AIM_ORIGIN_Y` = −35) to the cursor's **look-ahead-free world point** `cursor_aim_world = f_q + (cursor_vp − picture_centre) / k_eff`, where `f_q` is the pipeline's **published** follow pose **before** look-ahead, the inner-box clamp and jolt (pipeline D.6's `f`, floored to the camera quantum; pipeline D.14b publishes it every frame), `cursor_vp` the cursor in **physical window px** relative to the world picture's origin, `picture_centre = (L_w, L_h) · k_z / 2` in the same unit and `k_eff = k_z` the physical px per base px (the unit is the pipeline's D.14b `cursor_px`; the root viewport mouse position is already physical window px because root stretch is disabled (ADR-0008 § 5), so the Adapter reads it with no inverse stretch transform; the pipeline's in-engine acceptance criterion in "Camera follow, look-ahead, cuts" only confirms it) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*. **Who subtracts the letterbox offset:** the raw window-px value is *not* picture-relative; the Adapter's aim path forms `cursor_vp = cursor_window_px − RenderLayout.world_rect.position` (the world picture's top-left, D.14b `picture_origin`; it equals `picture_rect.position` whenever the world fills the picture, as at the default step) exactly once, then applies `(cursor_vp − picture_centre) / k_eff`. If it instead calls `RenderLayout.mouse_to_world_aim(cursor_px, f_q)` with the raw window px, that method performs the same single subtraction (D.14b `c_pic = cursor_px − picture_origin`) and the Adapter subtracts nothing. Never both; the Chart Table layer's own `picture_rect.position` subtraction (PC-2a) is a separate path that never feeds aim. With a non-zero letterbox, skipping the subtraction offsets the beam by `offset / k_eff` base px, so AC-PCI-056 and AC-PCI-059 use a letterboxed window *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: ADR-0008 § 3.3, § 5; the Adapter-forwarded raw value left the picture-origin subtraction unowned)*; inside `AIM_MOUSE_DEADZONE_PX` = 8 base px of the aim origin the aim holds, **measured in picture space with the camera settled** (the near-body fixtures in Acceptance Criteria are authored that way; while the follow lag is non-zero the cursor-to-origin distance carries the lag term below). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4: the Controller's form is canonical and the pipeline publishes `f_q`, a contract, no longer a request; the review's alternative of anchoring on the avatar `p = floor(pos_curr)` was not adopted, see the trade-off below)* **Why not C11's `cursor_world`:** C11 maps through `pose_q`, which includes the look-ahead `L`, and `L` is a function of this very aim; the loop (aim moves camera, camera moves the world point under a still cursor, which moves aim) would slew the beam towards a near cursor and keep it drifting after the mouse stops, with no cursor drawn to explain it. Measuring against `f_q` breaks the loop: `f_q` depends on the avatar's motion only, so with the avatar still a still cursor gives a constant `aim_q` while the look-ahead settles, and the beam still goes where the hidden cursor is *relative to the avatar*. The Adapter reads exactly two presentation values from the pipeline for this: `f_q` (published, pipeline D.14b) and the screen→picture mapping (`picture_centre`, `k_eff`); it reads no sim block for it beyond `body.state.pos_curr`. **Recorded trade-off** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)*: the aim is `cursor_aim_world − AIM_ORIGIN = (f_q − floor(pos_curr)) + cursor_off − (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)`, so it is a function of the cursor, the follow lag `f_q − pos` and `k_eff`. With the avatar still the lag is zero and a still mouse gives a constant aim whatever the look-ahead does; **when a run starts or stops, `S = f_q − floor(pos_curr)` changes by at most 36 px at `v_run` (24 at walk): the lag part ≤ 32.4 px (the dt→0 bound; 30.9 px at 60 fps), the tick phase ≤ 3 px, floor 1 px (pipeline D.6, D.14b; D.7 variable `f_q`), and a still mouse turns the beam by the geometry of that shift** (about 31°, `atan(36/60)`, for a cursor 60 px from the avatar at full shift; about 10°, `atan(36/200)`, at 200 px), with no input from the player *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. This is accepted because the reference the hidden cursor is read against is the *picture* (the camera's follow pose), which is also what the player sees move; the review's alternative of anchoring on the avatar position would hold the beam rigid against the hand but put the hidden-cursor reference up to 104 px (32.4 px of lag plus the 72 px look-ahead) off the drawn avatar. Neither form puts the reference on the drawn avatar; the cost of the chosen form is bounded and is measured by the target-acquisition item of the motion-integrity spike (Game Feel, "Target acquisition protocol"), with pre-recorded fallbacks. Pillar 1's "the beam is your hand" is protected where it matters most: the beam never moves when the avatar is still, and its origin and shape are untouched. The beam is **drawn** from the real `CHEST_POINT` (PC-5 below); only the *measurement* uses the fixed reference, so a facing flip (the chest point moves up to 4 px horizontally, more vertically across rows) can never move the aim, and the sheet/flip/beam limit cycle with a near cursor cannot occur. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: loop break and facing-independent aim origin; the 2026-10-26 user decision "mouse aim is the direction from the floored chest point to the cursor" is honoured as the **reference chest** — a presentation-neutral measurement point that does not change what the player sees or does; the beam origin is still the true chest point. Confirmed 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D10, this reading of the decision is accepted: a fixed reference chest `AIM_ORIGIN` measures the aim, the beam is drawn from the true chest point. Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Gamepad right stick: **radial dead-zone `AIM_DEADZONE` = 0.20 (0.10–0.35), scaled-radial, outer `AIM_OUTER` = 0.90, curve exponent `AIM_CURVE` = 1.0; no magnetism, no snap, no slow-down near targets, no input-side smoothing by default**. Keyboard: the arrow keys and `aim_hold` give octant-centre aim (PC-2). **Released-stick behaviour is one setting with three states, `aim_release_mode` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: replaces the binary `aim_follows_movement` knob; folds amendment (9))*:**

| `aim_release_mode` | A released stick (aim-idle) … | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `FOLLOW_MOVE_WHEN_RELEASED` | slews towards the movement octant centre at `AIM_FOLLOW_SLEW` (default 64 units/tick = 5.6°/tick, so a 180° swing takes 32 ticks; the lamp never whips and never stays stuck on the last door); holds when standing still; any right-stick deflection takes over at once | **the default** *(Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D8, Open Question 21. Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `HOLD` | holds the last aim forever; until the **first** right-stick deflection of the session the aim follows the movement octant centre (amendment (9): so a pad player who never aims is not stuck pointing south) | an option in Settings (the previously decided amendment (9), no longer the default; it brings back the lamp stuck on a door once the player has aimed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `FOLLOW_ALWAYS` | the stick is never live; `aim_angle` = movement octant centre on every octant change (one `set_aim` per change); standing still holds; mouse motion takes over | the one-handed preset |

In all three modes a deflected stick is absolute-direction aim and wins immediately; `live` is false whenever the stick is released or in `FOLLOW_ALWAYS`. *Two consumers.* The **raw aim** is read locally by the Avatar Presenter (the local beam's `Light2D` rotation, continuous, in the same frame — lights may rotate off-grid, PR-4 rule 5) and by the camera look-ahead (C4, regardless of lit state); the look-ahead consumes the *angle* of the raw aim measured against `f_q`, so it feeds on a quantity it cannot move *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, **weighted toward the movement direction while running** by the pipeline's own D.7 formula (D6 option B: `w = LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT · smoothstep(V_WALK, V_RUN, |v|)`, default 0.50, owned and tuned by the pipeline; D.7b cites it and defines nothing of its own, so walking, standing, carrying and channelling keep the pure aim look-ahead and the boarding-rhythm sweep is unchanged) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; re-review, general rule G1: the Controller-side formula and knob `RUN_LOOKAHEAD_MOVE_WEIGHT` are withdrawn)*. The **committed aim** (`aim_angle`, 1/4096 turn, ≤ 1 tick late, ≥ 1/128-turn send threshold) is what Emitter State orients the beam emitter by for the exposure query, what Interaction's focus weighting uses, what facing derives from, and what co-op replicates. A ≤ 2.8° / ≤ 1-tick divergence between the drawn and simulated cone is accepted; the identity channel (Pillar 1) is shape and colour, which come from the replicated loadout, never from aim.

*Cursor warp and source handover (one Adapter rule, presentation-local).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the 35 px origin offset does not scale with `k`, so a zoom step turned the beam under a still mouse, and the first mouse movement after stick use snapped the beam to a stale cursor position; the earlier acceptance criteria allowed both.)* The OS cursor is hidden and confined in `gameplay`, so the Adapter may reposition it without the player seeing a pointer move. **(i) Zoom edge:** on a `zoom_in` / `zoom_out` step (the pipeline's `RESCALING` frame) the Adapter warps the hidden cursor to `picture_centre + cursor_off_old × k_eff_new`, keeping `cursor_off` (base px) and therefore `aim_q` unchanged within ±1 unit; if the target position falls outside the window the warp clamps and the aim may differ (the confinement rectangle is the window, Edge Cases). **(ii) Handover to the mouse:** while the aim source is STICK or KEYS the mouse takes over only after `AIM_MOUSE_TAKEOVER_PX` (default 6 physical window px, **[PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]**, range 2–16) of accumulated cursor travel, so a nudged desk does not hijack the lamp; at takeover the Adapter first warps the cursor to the point whose direction from `AIM_ORIGIN` is the current raw aim and whose radius is `max(last |r|, AIM_WARP_RADIUS_PX)` (default 48 base px, **[PLACEHOLDER]**, range 16–96), so the first mouse sample holds the aim it found instead of jumping to a stale position. **(iii) `aim_source` ∈ {AUTO, MOUSE, STICK, KEYS}:** `AUTO` (default) follows the last aim axis, with (ii) applied on a handover to the mouse; `MOUSE`, `STICK` or `KEYS` force one source and ignore the others (no handover, no warp on the forced source; the mouse stays warped only for (i)). A warp is Adapter-local presentation: it submits nothing, writes no block and reveals no sim state.

*Beam shapes are a loadout, not an input.* The four shapes (wide, narrow, split, fan — art bible 3.2, 8.3) are a per-player Suit & Equipment fact; there is no "change shape" action. `beam_toggle` submits **`toggle_beam`** (owner: Emitter State; actor = the seat's beam emitter ID). The beam is an emitter like any other for Attention, which is what makes "go dark" a strategy.

*Acknowledgement vs the anchor.* The beam is a human-channel anchor that may never lie. If the local light lit on the press and the sim then rejected (`CHARGE_EMPTY`), the beam would flicker once on the one object that may not. Rule: **the toggle's sound and the hand's switch animation pre-play immediately; the light itself changes only on committed state** (≤ 17 ms later, imperceptible against a 3-screen-px cone edge). The Avatar Presenter never predicts beam state. **Pose and value split (LO-2):** the Presenter publishes the local beam pose — position and rotation only — and 2D Lighting & Occlusion writes the value members (`enabled`, `energy`, `texture`, `color`) from `emitter.state`; the Presenter's pose write precedes Lighting's apply in the LIGHTING present step (ADR-0009 § 3) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync)*. **Toggle rate limit (accessibility, WCAG 2.3.1):** the Adapter submits no `toggle_beam` within `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` = 12 ticks of its previous submitted `toggle_beam`; a press inside the window is dropped (no pre-play, no click, nothing submitted), so input can flip the beam at most five times (2.5 flashes) per second, inside the WCAG 2.3.1 limit of three with a margin (the earlier 10 ticks allowed exactly three flashes per second, at the limit with none) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: 10 → 12; the value also stops the Adapter permitting a toggle that Emitter State's own 12-tick switch cooldown would answer `TOO_SOON`)*. The 12-tick floor also covers Suit's six-tick (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) sampled-drain window (SE-35). A sim-side guard in `toggle_beam.validate` is Emitter State's to add (Open Questions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion, emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query, suit-and-equipment)*

*Chest point (beam origin) and hand point (tool grip).* The beam's origin is `floor(interp_pos) + CHEST_POINT[facing][animation]`, mirrored as `(−x, y)` for the three mirrored facings, using the same floored position as the sprite (PR-4 rule 2) so light origin and sprite never disagree; the table is per facing **and per animation** (run lean, carry and stagger frames move it slightly), and art keeps `|x| ≤ 2 px` so a mirror barely moves the light (registry `chest_point`; user decision 2026-10-26 — chest-mounted, hands-free lamp). `HAND_POINT[facing][animation]` is the separate tool-grip anchor and carries no light. The Controller Sim publishes both points in its read view so that when the Perception Filter substitutes a horror rig (class c, art bible 5.0) the beam still originates at the *true* chest point. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*

**PC-6. One focus model, interaction reach, lit-only actions, hold-to-confirm.** *Reach* is a sector from the foot origin: `REACH_PX` = 72 base px (1.5 tiles — the adjacent ring including diagonals at 68 px), half-angle `REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` = 70° about the **facing**. Objects behind the player are never candidates. *Focus — the same model on every device:* among interactables that are in reach, in the arc and **lit** (Emitter State's exposure query above the technical-artist's threshold), the focused one minimises `score = d / REACH_PX + FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT × θ / REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG`, with `θ` the angle between the object's direction from the foot origin and the **ground-projected aim** (mouse: the direction from the foot origin to `cursor_aim_world`, PC-5; stick and keys: the committed aim, a direction only) and `FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT` = 1.0 (0.5–2.0), `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` = 0.10 (D.9) — **aim-weighted proximity** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: measuring θ from the chest-height aim origin made a floor object the player points straight at read 30° off; the focus score is Adapter-side presentation, so the ground projection changes no sim value, and `in_reach` is untouched)*; the mouse has no separate pick-under-cursor path (the cursor moves the aim, and aim weights the score, so the model is one). Exactly one object is focused; the HUD draws its prompt (`E · Cut`) only when lit (art bible 7.0), at `world_to_hud(target)`; an off-screen object has no prompt (C9). **Self-target rule** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP, blocker PC-6 focus model)*: the candidate set has one fallback member, the actor's own suit (object class `ACTOR_SELF`). It is nominated if and only if (i) Suit's `tool_in_hand(player_id)` (the actor's own hand rail, SE-40, read like `carried_instance`) names a tool for which Interaction's declaration data has an `ACTOR_SELF` row (today `tool_welder`, the self-patch of IA-17; matched on the exact tool only, IA-5 tier (1), no `NEEDS_TOOL` fallback), (ii) `carry_ref == none` and `loco_state ∉ {DOWNED, DEAD, IN_POD}`, and (iii) the world candidate set (in reach, in arc, lit) is empty. It is the focus only while (iii) holds: any world candidate displaces it on the frame that candidate appears, and `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` never holds the self-target against a world candidate. Its score is `SELF_FOCUS_SCORE = 2 + FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT × 180 / REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG + FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` (D.9), load-computed and not a knob (4.67 at defaults), so it is above every world score. The Adapter submits `begin_patch {target_ref = own PlayerId}`, mode `CHANNEL`, under the PC-7 hold policy. No lit or reach test applies (the row is `act_gate`-exempt, IA-6), and the Controller never reads `integrity_bp` or any suit reading to decide that the self-target exists: a healthy suit is Interaction's `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` outcome (IA-17), so there is no oracle. An unlit object, a phantom and an empty floor all leave the world set empty and give the identical self-target (Pillar 4). `focus_view.self_target` is true (Interaction IA-41) and the HUD draws the plate at the chest keep-out (HS-37, HS-39); the lit rule is not applied to it. Consequence, accepted: with the welder in hand and any lit candidate in reach and arc, the world candidate wins; the player turns the facing away from it (mouse: aim away; pad: move or aim away) to reach the self-prompt. **The Adapter never chooses the verb**: `interact` is one button, and **Interaction declares, per focused object and equipped tool, the command type and its mode** ∈ {`TAP`, `HOLD_CONFIRM`, `CHANNEL`}; the Adapter applies the matching policy over the same commands. **Decision (art bible 7.0 leaves it to the game-designer): an action is possible only on a lit object.** Interaction's `validate` calls the exposure query the prompt uses, so the prompt never lies by omission. *Rationale:* "what you light, you can read" must also be "what you light, you can touch", or working in the dark becomes a free Attention dodge and "go dark" degenerates into the dominant strategy; light has to cost Attention for darkness to be a trade. Exceptions are Interaction's `act_gate` exempt rows (registry `act_gate`): `drop` of the carried module, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card of an already-lit instrument, `throw_glowstick` and, by the resolution below, every `ACTOR_SELF` verb. **Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling G1 with the re-review's recommendation (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): option (a), `ACTOR_SELF` verbs are an `act_gate` exempt row (Open Question 16).** The self-patch (`begin_patch` on object class `ACTOR_SELF`, Interaction IA-17) is the only MVP way to repair the suit (Suit SE-22). As Interaction, Emitter State and the registry stood it could never pass the lit check: Emitter State EM-21 adds the owner's own beam to the owner's `lux_pm` only and states it "is not added to the owner's `act_lux_pm`" (the player is never the target of their own verb), the `lit` flag is computed from `act_lux_pm`, and Interaction listed `NOT_LIT` for `begin_patch`, so a player alone with the lamp on got `NOT_LIT`. "What you light, you can touch" was never meant to read "you cannot patch your own suit in the dark"; read that way the Pillar 3 "go dark" lever would be a trap. So `ACTOR_SELF` verbs join the exempt rows beside `drop`, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card and `throw_glowstick`; EM-21's "never the target of your own verb" stays intact everywhere else. The "known defect" wording is withdrawn. Consequences stated here so the siblings can conform: the lamp-off result is `Applied` (patching yourself in the dark is legal), and the mid-channel unlit re-check of PC-7 (v) skips a channel whose target class is `ACTOR_SELF`, because there is no lit object to lose. **Siblings conformed 2026-10-28 (delegated G6):** Interaction IA-6 / D.2 / IA-7 / IA-14 / IA-17 and the registry `act_gate` (owner Interaction) exempt the `ACTOR_SELF` self-patch as an actor verb, and Emitter State EM-21 is unchanged and notes why. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review, delegated ruling G1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6; conformance)* Web Map & Transit's request to exempt `pod_hop` from the lit conjunct (ASK-I2) is Interaction's to accept in `act_gate`; `in_reach` (D.9) keeps its lit conjunct. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from web-map-and-transit)* *Hold-to-confirm* (`HOLD_CONFIRM` mode — destructive verbs, HR-14): a **local** hold of `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` = 0.8 s (0.6–1.2) in render time, drawn on the art bible 7.3 **linear bar — never a ring** (routed correction to the kernel's "progress ring" wording); **release drains the bar at 2×**; on completion one command is submitted; the sim never sees a partial hold. Option: **tap-to-confirm** — two taps, arm then confirm, within `CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW` = 3 s. No QTEs anywhere. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; D.10 has the arithmetic.)* **Hardening:** (i) each frame adds at most `CONFIRM_DT_MAX_S` = 0.05 s to the bar, so a single 1 s hitch cannot confirm a destructive cut; (ii) the target is **latched** while the bar is above zero: a focus change during a hold neither transfers nor completes the bar, it drains at 2× on the latched target and the new focus starts from zero after release; (iii) modes are exclusive per declaration (IA-8): a `HOLD_CONFIRM` verb submits one `TAP`-class command on completion and never a `begin_*`; if a future verb needs confirm-then-work, Interaction declares it as a `CHANNEL` with a confirm step, the completed bar submits `begin_<verb>`, the plate resets to zero and the HUD re-fills it from the channel's committed progress, and the held `interact` then sustains the channel in hold mode (the "release before re-arm" rule belongs to the confirm bar only); (iv) the tap-window timer is frozen while the kernel is `PAUSED` and disarms on context change.

**PC-7. Channels (cut / weld / patch).** `interact` on an object whose declared mode is `CHANNEL` → the Adapter submits `begin_<verb> {target}` (`coalesce = NONE`; owner Interaction, Assembly behind it for weld/cut). The owner's `validate` requires: lit and in reach, except an `ACTOR_SELF` channel (no lit or reach test, PC-6) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6; conformance)*, **`controller.state.intent_octant == NONE`**, not carrying, not staggered/downed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `intent_octant` and `intent_run` are written by the `set_move_intent` `apply` handler in P2; `octant` is the value `controller.step` publishes in P3 and the channel owners' hooks read. Validation that read `octant` saw last tick's value, so releasing W and pressing E in one frame was rejected, and pressing W and E together was accepted and then aborted silently. With the split the first applies and the second is rejected at once with reason `MOVING` (immediate class, "no" cue; proposed to Interaction, today folded into `BUSY` — Open Question 25). Both rely on the Adapter submitting intents before commands in a frame (PC-2).)* **Progress is sim state in `interaction.state`** (with `owner_system` ASSEMBLY for weld and section cut), accrued per tick by Interaction's hook; the owner's `apply` records `channel_ref`, `channel_face` and `channel_body` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6; INTERNAL fix)* through the Controller's `set_channel_ref(actor, owner_system, channel_id, face_octant)` mutator (PC-1 b) and the Controller holds facing at `channel_face` (PC-4 rule 2); `loco_state` becomes `CHANNELLING` in that tick's `controller.step` by the precedence function of States and Transitions *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from modular-ship-assembly, interaction-system)* **Hold is the default; a toggle option exists; both are Adapter policy over the same two commands.** *Interrupts:* (i) **release** (hold mode) or a second press / `cancel` (toggle mode) → `cancel_channel`; (ii) **movement** — `controller.step` itself clears `channel_ref` and `channel_face` in the tick an applied intent has `octant ≠ NONE` (step 3 of the `controller.step` order, so the move wins on that same tick and the first movement step is not lost), and the owner's hook, declared `after: controller.step`, reads `controller.state.octant` and aborts (sim-side, so identical in `MIRROR`; the 8-way dead-zone already filters stick drift) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (iii) **stagger / downed** — the Controller Sim clears `channel_ref`; (iv) **target invalidated** (power drops mid-cut, the section detaches) — the owner aborts and reports through state change, never through the command result (CR-5); (v) **unlit** — the owner re-checks the target's `lit` every `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` (6, Interaction IA-7) and aborts `UNLIT` (not for a channel whose target class is `ACTOR_SELF`: that verb is `act_gate`-exempt, PC-6, Open Question 16 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); **proposed grace** (accessibility; Interaction's to adopt, Open Question 25): `LIT_ABORT_GRACE_TICKS` = 30 (0–60, extendable by an accessibility setting) is a counter to which each consecutive dark check adds `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` (so 30 = five consecutive dark checks) and a lit check resets, so tremor, stick drift or sliding off a narrow beam edge no longer aborts a 360-tick weld while a lamp that is switched off still aborts within half a second *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (vi) a body transfer (step 1's body edge: the stamped `channel_body` no longer equals `body_of_actor`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6; INTERNAL fix)*, a restore (snapshot normalisation), and entry to `chart_table` or `chat` (Contexts, PC-2). The player keeps control of the body at all times: a channel never locks movement, movement cancels the channel. *Rationale:* Dread Before Chaos needs "I can leave now" to be true in every state. **Pause:** the tick stops, progress freezes; on resume the hold survives if the button is still down (PC-2 resync). Whether partial progress persists on the target after an abort is **Interaction's rule** (IA-16).
