| f_q | vec2 float | multiples of the camera quantum | calculated (pipeline D.6 follow term, before look-ahead, inner-box clamp and jolt; **published by the pipeline** every frame, D.14b, delegated ruling R4) | The camera position the picture would have with `L = 0`; a function of the avatar's motion only, never of aim. It trails the avatar by the follow lag `lag_ss(dt)` (0 at rest; 30.9 px at 60 fps, 31.4 at 90, 31.8 at 144 at `v_run`; 32.4 px = `v_run · τ_follow` is the dt→0 upper bound, pipeline D.6) plus the interpolation phase `(1 − tick_alpha) · v / TICK_RATE` ≤ 3 px (2 px at walk), because `f` follows the interpolated target while `AIM_ORIGIN` uses the committed `floor(pos_curr)`; this is the recorded trade-off of PC-5. Derived: `S = f_q − floor(pos_curr) = floor(−(lag_ss(dt) + (1 − tick_alpha_draw) · v_tick))` per axis, `|S|` ≤ 36 px at the run and ≤ 24 at the walk (floor of 32.4 + 3 = 35.4 → 36; 21.6 + 2 = 23.6 → 24; pipeline D.14b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| cursor_vp, picture_centre, k_eff | vec2 float / float | physical window px (`cursor_vp`, `picture_centre`); physical px per base px (`k_eff = k_z`) | calculated (Adapter reads the root viewport mouse position, already physical window px with root stretch disabled, ADR-0008 § 5; no inverse stretch transform; then subtracts `RenderLayout.world_rect.position` (D.14b `picture_origin`) exactly once to form the picture-relative `cursor_vp`, or lets `RenderLayout.mouse_to_world_aim` do that one subtraction; pipeline D.14b publishes `f_q` and the mapping) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)*. The unit is stated by ADR-0008, not **[UNV]**; the pipeline's in-engine criterion in "Camera follow, look-ahead, cuts" (cursor one screen px east gives `cursor_off.x = 1 / k_z`) only confirms it *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* | Cursor position relative to the world picture's origin, picture centre, and the scale; `(cursor_vp − picture_centre) / k_eff` is the pipeline's `cursor_off` in base px *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* |
| aim_origin | vec2 int | — | calculated | Fixed reference chest: `pos_curr + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)`, independent of facing, sheet and animation row |
| AIM_ORIGIN_Y *(design review)* | int | −45 … −25 (**−35**) base px | data file | Reference chest height above the foot origin; mid-range of `CHEST_POINT.y`; the beam is still **drawn** from the true `CHEST_POINT` (D.12) |
| AIM_MOUSE_DEADZONE_PX | int | 4–16 (**8**) base px | data file | Cursor radius around the aim origin inside which aim holds (a cursor on the lamp has no direction) |
| AIM_DEADZONE | float | 0.10–0.35 (**0.20**) | Settings | Right-stick radial deadzone; below it the stick is aim-idle |
| AIM_OUTER | float | 0.80–1.00 (**0.90**) | data file | Deflection treated as full |
| AIM_CURVE | float | 1.0–2.0 (**1.0**) | Settings | Response exponent on the scaled-radial magnitude |
| m' | float | 0–1 | calculated | Scaled-radial deflection strength |
| AIM_UNITS | int | **4096** | constant (set-once) | Units per turn of `aim_angle`; a multiple of 8 so every octant centre (`512 × o`) and boundary (`512 × o ± 256`) is an integer |
| aim_q | int | 0–4095 | calculated | Quantised aim, the payload of `set_aim` and the value of `controller.state.aim_angle` once applied |
| last_acked | int | 0–4095 | calculated (Adapter; set on submit, reverted on any rejection so the next frame resends) | Last value the Adapter believes the sim holds |
| Δ | int | 0–2048 | calculated | Circular distance in aim units |
| AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD | int | 16–64 (**32** = 1/128 turn = 2.8125°) | data file | Change that triggers a send |
| AIM_SEND_INTERVAL | int | 1–6 (**3**) ticks | data file | Minimum ticks between sends |
| AIM_SETTLE_TICKS | int | 3–12 (**6**) | data file | Consecutive ticks with `0 < Δ < threshold` after which the residual is sent |
| settle_ticks | int | 0–AIM_SETTLE_TICKS | calculated | Consecutive-tick counter; reset on send or on `Δ = 0` |
| source_changed | bool | — | calculated | Aim source flipped between mouse, stick and keys this frame (`aim_source` ∈ {AUTO, MOUSE, STICK, KEYS}; bypasses the threshold, not the interval) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| live | bool | — | calculated | Mouse: always true; stick: `m ≥ AIM_DEADZONE`; keys: any aim key held; `aim_release_mode = FOLLOW_ALWAYS`: always false. Travels in the payload because PC-4 rules 4–6 branch on it in the sim *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26)* |
| live_changed | bool | — | calculated | `live` differs from the last sent value — a stick release or first deflection sends even though the angle is unchanged (bypasses the threshold, not the interval); without it a release would leave the sim on rule 4 forever |
| send | bool | — | calculated | Whether a `set_aim` is submitted this frame |

**Output Range:** Boolean; `aim_q` is exact to 360 / 4096 = 0.088°; the committed cone may lag the drawn (raw) cone by at most `AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD` = 2.8° and by at most one tick plus the interval wait (≤ 3 ticks = 50 ms during a continuous sweep, 1 tick on a settle) — the accepted divergence in PC-5. Send rate: 0 at rest; ≤ `TICK_RATE / AIM_SEND_INTERVAL` = **20 sends/s** during a sweep; a settle send fires once and then `Δ = 0`. A full-rate sweep of 360° in 1 s produces 20 sends of 18° each — far above the threshold, each applied within ≤ 3 ticks. **`AIM_OUTER` and `AIM_CURVE` are dormant under absolute-direction aim**: direction is `atan2` of the stick and does not depend on `m'`; they affect nothing unless a consumer reads `aim_strength` (candidate: the camera look-ahead scaling `offset = aim_dir × m' × LOOKAHEAD_PX` — the pipeline's decision; registry `camera_lookahead` uses a unit `aim_dir` today). Finding routed to Section C and the pipeline; the knobs are kept in Section G as *dormant* so they are not mistaken for live tuning. The measurement uses the fixed `aim_origin`, so a facing flip (the true chest point moves ≤ 4 px horizontally, more across rows, D.12) changes nothing in `aim_q`; the sheet/flip/beam limit cycle with a cursor 10–40 px from the body cannot occur, and the mouse deadzone (8 base px = 32 screen px at k = 4) is only ever crossed by the cursor. Because the cursor point is taken against `f_q`, **a stationary mouse with a still avatar gives a constant `aim_q` while the look-ahead settles** (Acceptance Criteria: ±1 unit over 120 ticks at 20, 60 and 200 px); when the avatar starts or stops moving, `aim_q` equals the value the formula computes from the current `f_q − pos_curr` within ±1 (the lag drift of PC-5, a recorded trade-off, not a defect), and a zoom step changes it only through the `k_eff` division (D.7 variable table; Edge Cases, zoom). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review, delegated ruling R4)*
**Example:** `pos_curr = (292, 234)` and `AIM_ORIGIN_Y = −35` give `aim_origin = (292, 199)`; the follow pose is settled on the avatar (`f_q = (292, 234)`) and the cursor sits at picture offset `(8, −54)` base px from the centre, so `cursor_aim_world = (300, 180)` → `r = (8, −19)`, `|r| = 20.6 ≥ 8` → `θ_raw = atan2(19, 8) = 67.166°` → `aim_q = posmod(round(67.166 × 4096 / 360), 4096) = round(764.23) = 764` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier 765 came from rounding twice, 67.2° then 764.6)*. `last_acked = 740` → `Δ = 24 < 32`; T − last_send = 5 ≥ 3 → no send; `settle_ticks` counts 1 … 6 → on the sixth tick a send of 764 (if the cursor is still). The next frame the cursor moves to `θ = 75°` → `aim_q = 853`, `Δ = 89 ≥ 32` → send immediately if the interval allows. **Lower half-plane (wrap):** `r = (8, 19)` → `θ_raw = atan2(−19, 8) = −67.166°` → `round(−764.23) = −764` → `posmod(−764, 4096) = 3332`; due south `r = (0, 40)` → `θ_raw = −90°` → `−1024` → `3072`; `θ_raw = −0.05°` → `round(−0.569) = −1` → `4095`. (`θ_raw = −0.01°` rounds to `−0` = 0, not 4095: round first, then wrap.) A stick flick from `aim_q = 100` to `2148` → `Δ = min(2048, 2048) = 2048` → one send. Stick at `m = 0.5`: `m' = ((0.5 − 0.2) / 0.7)^1.0 = 0.43` — published, consumed by nothing at MVP.

**D.7b Run-weighted camera look-ahead (presentation only)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D6 option B, Open Question 19; rewritten 2026-10-27 — re-review, general rule G1 (the owner wins): the pipeline owns the camera, so the formula and the knob live there and this section cites them)*

The Controller defines **no look-ahead formula and no look-ahead knob of its own.** User ruling D6 option B (while running, the camera look-ahead is weighted toward the movement direction; camera only, the beam stays honest and the sim is untouched) is implemented once, in the pipeline's D.7 `look_ahead` (registry `camera_lookahead`): `L_target = L_MAX_eff · ((1 − w) · m · û(aim) + w · û_v)` with `w = LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT · smoothstep(V_WALK, V_RUN, |v|)`; `LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT` (alias `W_RUN`) defaults to **0.50**, safe range 0–0.75, `[PLACEHOLDER]`, and `v` is the avatar's committed displacement per tick (`pos_curr − pos_prev`, the read the follow target already makes). The earlier Controller-side knob `RUN_LOOKAHEAD_MOVE_WEIGHT` (default 0.75, range 0.55–0.90, keyed on `loco_state == RUN`) and its constraints C1–C3 are **withdrawn** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G1)*: the two formulas differed in default, range, key and acceptance numbers (+36 px against 0), the Controller's old C2 (`w > 0.5`) rejected the pipeline's default, and both could not be built. What stays on the Controller's side, and is tested here:

- **The weight is keyed on the gait the player chose, never on rhythm.** `w` is a function of the avatar's committed speed only; nothing reads Attention, rhythm, sanity or the beam's lit state, so Pillar 2's design test (the same input gives the same movement and beam motion in both rhythms) is untouched.
- **The sim is untouched.** `aim_angle`, the beam's `Light2D` rotation, `facing` and every sim block are bit-identical for any `LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT`; only where the picture is centred changes.
- **Inner-box safety (C1, kept).** `(1 − w) · m · û(aim) + w · û_v` is a convex combination of vectors of length ≤ 1 for `w` in [0, 1], so `|L_target| ≤ L_MAX_eff` and the inner-box inequality of D.2 (32.4 + 72 = 104.4 ≤ 108) holds unchanged; no new invariant is needed.

**Variables:** none are the Controller's; `LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT`, `V_WALK`, `V_RUN`, `L_MAX_eff` and the smoothstep belong to the pipeline (D.7, Tuning Knobs, registry). The Controller supplies `pos_prev`, `pos_curr` (through Body) and the raw aim.

**Output Range:** `|L_target| ≤ L_MAX_eff` always. At the pipeline's 0.50 and a full run, an aim straight back gives `L_target = 0`: the camera sits on the follow pose instead of leaning 72 px behind, so turning your back on the pursuer costs the view of it by presentation alone (the D6 intent), and the lean never leans *forward* of the avatar for a backwards aim. Entering and leaving a run eases through the pipeline's `L_SLEW_MAX`.
**Example** (pipeline numbers, `m = 1`): running E, `û_v = (1, 0)`, `w = 0.50`. Aim E: `L_target` = 72 px ahead. Aim NE (screen y down, `û_aim = (0.707, −0.707)`): `û_look = 0.5 × (0.707, −0.707) + 0.5 × (1, 0) = (0.854, −0.354)`, magnitude 0.924, **66.5 px**. Aim W: `0.5 × (−1, 0) + 0.5 × (1, 0) = (0, 0)`, **0 px**, where the unweighted look-ahead would have put the camera 72 px behind. The same aim W while walking (`w = 0`): 72 px behind.
**Hand-off to the pipeline's owner (cross-GDD note, not edited here):** (1) the pipeline keys `w` on `|v|` through `smoothstep(2, 3, |v|)` over integer displacements; a *walking* diagonal step of 2 px per axis has `|v| = 2.83` and gives `r ≈ 0.93`, so a diagonal walk leans toward the movement on those ticks and the cadence (1, 1, 2, 1, 2 …, D.3) makes `w` flicker. A `loco_state == RUN` key (the Controller already publishes `loco_state` in its `SIM` read view) removes both defects and is the review's recommendation; (2) the old Controller bound `w > 0.5` (aim back never leans the camera behind the avatar) sits exactly on the pipeline's default 0.50 and is lost below it, so the pipeline's safe range should start at 0.50 if the D6 intent is to hold.

**D.8 Facing from committed aim — sector hysteresis and overrides**

The `facing` formula (registry: `facing_from_aim`; `controller.step`, every tick, no easing; rules evaluated top-down, first match wins) is defined as:

`facing = facing_prev if loco_state ∈ {STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD} (rule 1, frozen);  (channel_face if channel_face ≠ none else facing_prev) if loco_state == CHANNELLING (rule 2: the stored octant written by `set_channel_ref`; `channel_face = none`, the ACTOR_SELF self-patch, holds `facing_prev`, so the body neither turns to the aim nor to a target; rules 3 to 6 never apply while CHANNELLING; the chest lamp still follows aim);  octant if loco_state == RUN (rule 3);  f_aim if aim_live (rule 4);  octant if octant ≠ NONE (rule 5);  facing_prev otherwise (rule 6)` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, blocker PC-1(b))*

Derived: `f_aim_raw = posmod(floor((aim_angle + 256) / 512), 8)` (`posmod`, as every `mod` in D.7 and D.8)
Derived: `f_aim = f_aim_raw if cdist(aim_angle, 512 × facing_prev) > 256 + FACING_HYSTERESIS, else facing_prev`
Derived: `sheet = facing ∈ {3, 4, 5} ? mirror_of(facing) : facing; flip_h = facing ∈ {3, 4, 5}` (NW→NE, W→E, SW→SE)
Derived: `octant_of(r)` for an integer vector `r = anchor_o − foot` (computed by the channel owner at the `begin_*` apply and passed as `face_octant`, PC-1 b): NONE if `r = (0, 0)`; otherwise the octant `o ∈ 0..7` that maximises `dot(UNIT[o], r)` with `UNIT` of D.3 (integers only, no trig), ties to the smaller `o`. Worked: `r = (−40, 30)` gives dot values SW(5) = 181·40 + 181·30 = 12670, W(4) = 10240, S(6) = 7680 → 5 (SW); `r = (60, −10)` gives E(0) = 15360, NE(1) = 10860 + 1810 = 12670 → 0 (E); `r = (181, −75)` gives E = 46336 and NE = 32761 + 13575 = 46336, a tie → 0; `r = (0, 0)` → NONE. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, blocker PC-1(b))*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| aim_angle | int | 0–4095 | calculated (`controller.state`, last applied `set_aim`) | Committed aim |
| facing_prev | int | 0–7 | calculated (`controller.state.facing` last tick; spawn **6 = S**) | Previous facing |
| FACING_HYSTERESIS | int | 32–96 (**64** = 1/64 turn = 5.625°) | data file | Extra distance past a sector boundary (256 units = 22.5°) before facing flips; must be ≥ `AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD` (one send can never straddle it) and < 256 |
| aim_live | bool | — | calculated (`controller.state.aim_live`, carried on `set_aim` — D.7) | Mouse: always true; stick: deflected past `AIM_DEADZONE`; keys: an aim key held; `FOLLOW_ALWAYS`: never |
| octant | int | 0–8 | calculated | Movement octant |
| channel_face | int | 0–7 or none | stored (`controller.state.channel_face`, written by `set_channel_ref`'s `face_octant` = `octant_of(target anchor − foot)` computed by the channel owner at the `begin_*` apply; cleared with `channel_ref`) | The locked work-facing octant; stored data, so a MIRROR client reads it; none for ACTOR_SELF or a zero vector; then rule 2 yields `facing_prev` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, blocker PC-1(b))* |
| facing | int | 0–7 | calculated | Published facing |
| flip_h | bool | — | calculated (Presenter) | Mirror flag for the three mirrored facings |

**Output Range:** Eight values, every tick, from committed state only. The hysteresis band is 2 × 64 = 128 units = 11.25° wide centred on each boundary; a mouse resting on a boundary never flickers. Facing flips in a single tick (a 180° flick is one tick, D.7's send permitting). The overrides are evaluated top-down and the first match wins, so a runner's facing ignores aim entirely and a stagger freezes whatever was there; a channel whose `channel_face` is none (the ACTOR_SELF self-patch, or a zero vector) holds `facing_prev` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, blocker PC-1(b))*. `aim_live` must travel with `set_aim` as a boolean payload field *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; PC-4 rules 4–6 branch on it, so it is sim state, and the Adapter is the only thing that knows whether a stick is deflected)*. Co-op: `facing` itself is in `controller.state` and replicates; a MIRROR client never recomputes it from aim, which is why the replicated aim's coarsening bound (≤ `FACING_HYSTERESIS`) is about the *beam* agreeing with the facing, not the facing itself.
**Example:** `aim_angle = 764` (67.17°): `f_aim_raw = floor(1020 / 512) = 1 (NE)`; centre of NE is 512, `cdist(764, 512) = 252 ≤ 320` → if `facing_prev = NE` hold NE; if `facing_prev = E (0)`, `cdist(764, 0) = 764 > 320` → NE. Lower half-plane: `aim_angle = 3332` (SE of the avatar, −67.17°) → `f_aim_raw = posmod(floor(3588 / 512), 8) = 7 (SE)`; `aim_angle = 4095` → `floor(4351 / 512) = 8 → posmod 0 (E)`; due south `3072` → `floor(3328 / 512) = 6 (S)`. `aim_angle` is already `posmod`-wrapped by D.7, so `f_aim_raw` is never negative; `cdist` uses `posmod` too *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: 765 → 764 and the wrap examples)*. Mouse drifts to `aim_angle = 800` (70.3°): `cdist(800, 512) = 288 ≤ 320` → NE holds (raw would say N from 768). At 833 (73.2°): `289 → 321 > 320` → N. Running SW while aiming NE → `facing = 5 (SW)`, `sheet = SE`, `flip_h = true`; the beam light still rotates to 67° from the mirrored chest point.

**D.9 Reach test and focus score**

The `in_reach` formula (sim-side, Interaction's `validate`; integer-only) is defined as:

`in_reach(o) = d² ≤ REACH_PX²  ∧  D > 0  ∧  D² × 1000 ≥ COS2_REACH × 65536 × d²  ∧  lit(o)`

Derived: `r = anchor_o − foot; d² = r.x² + r.y²; D = dot(UNIT[facing], r)` (integer, 1/256 scale); `COS2_REACH = round(1000 × cos²(REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG)) = 117`
Derived (Adapter-side, per render frame, float permitted — it chooses a target, the sim re-checks it): `score(o) = d / REACH_PX + FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT × θ_a / REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` over candidates with `in_reach(o)`
Derived: `focus = argmin score`, kept on the current focus unless `score(best) < score(current) − FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` or the current focus is no longer a candidate; ties → smaller `θ_a`, then smaller entity id
Derived: `SELF_FOCUS_SCORE = 2 + FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT × 180 / REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG + FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN`; the self-target (PC-6) is a candidate only when no `in_reach` world candidate exists, and its sticky margin is not applied against a world candidate *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP, blocker PC-6 focus model)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| anchor_o | vec2 int | — | calculated (Interaction's per-object interaction anchor; default = footprint centre) | Where the object is "at" for reach |
| foot | vec2 int | — | calculated (`body.state.pos_curr`, aliased `pos`; horizontally centred, the foot box's top row) | Foot origin *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics)* |
| d | float / d² int | 0–REACH_PX | calculated | Distance to the anchor; the sim compares squares |
| REACH_PX | int | 48–96 (**72**) base px | data file | Reach radius — 1.5 tiles; includes the diagonal neighbour at 48√2 = 67.9 |
| UNIT[facing] | vec2 int | D.3 table | constant | Facing direction at 1/256 scale (angle `θ_f` is measured from it); the diagonal entries are 255.97/256 of unit length — a 0.02 % bias on the arc edge, accepted |
| D | int | — | calculated | `dot(UNIT[facing], r)`; all terms of `in_reach` are integers (64-bit: `D²` ≤ (256 × 96 × 2)² ≈ 2.4 × 10^9, × 1000 fits) |
| REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG | float | 45–90 (**70**) | data file | Half-angle of the reach sector about **facing** |
| θ_f | float | 0–180° | calculated | Angle between facing and `r` — the arc test |
| θ_a | float | 0–180° | calculated (Adapter) | Angle between the **ground-projected aim** and `r` — the score weight. Mouse: the direction from `foot` to `cursor_aim_world` (D.7), so pointing the lamp straight at a floor object scores θ_a ≈ 0 instead of the 30° the chest-height origin would give; stick and keys: the committed `aim_angle` (a direction only). Presentation-side only; `in_reach` and the sim never see it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| lit(o) | bool | — | calculated (Emitter State's exposure query ≥ the technical-artist's threshold) | Object is lit |
| FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT | float | 0.5–2.0 (**1.0**) | data file | Weight of aim against proximity |
| FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN | float | 0–0.25 (**0.10**) | data file | Score advantage a challenger needs to take focus *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; two near-equal candidates would otherwise trade the prompt every frame as the aim jitters within one quantum)* |
| in_reach | bool | — | calculated | Candidate predicate; also the sim's validation of the Adapter's chosen target |
| score | float | 0–≈ 2.4 at defaults while facing tracks aim; ≤ 3.57 in every state (RUN) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP, blocker PC-6 focus model)* | calculated | Lower is better |
| SELF_FOCUS_SCORE | float | load-computed: 2 + 2.5714 + 0.10 = 4.6714 at defaults; 2 + 2.0 × 180 / 45 + 0.25 = 10.25 at the knob extremes W = 2.0, H = 45°, M = 0.25 | calculated at load (not a knob) | Score of the PC-6 self-target; above every world score in every state (invariant 19) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP, blocker PC-6 focus model)* |

**Output Range:** `in_reach` is exact integer arithmetic (sim-safe, G3-pure): `d² ≤ 5184`; `D > 0` excludes everything at or behind 90°, and the squared-cosine test (cos² 70° = 0.117, multiplied through by 256² = 65536 so no division occurs) the rest of the arc — objects behind the player are never candidates. `score` is bounded by `d / 72 ≤ 1` plus `W × θ_a / 70`; because aim and facing can differ by up to 22.5° + 5.6° (D.8) while the arc extends 70° from facing, `θ_a` can reach ≈ 98° and the score ≈ 2.4 while facing tracks aim (up to 22.5° + 5.6° = 28.1° apart); under RUN (D.8 rule 3, facing = movement octant whatever the aim) `θ_a` can reach 180°, so the all-state bound is `1 + FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT × 180 / REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` = 1 + 2.5714 = 3.57 at defaults and 1 + 8 = 9.0 at W = 2.0, H = 45° *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP, blocker PC-6 focus model)* — it is a ranking, not a probability, and is never clamped. Minimum 0: an object at the foot on the aim line. The sim never computes `score`; it only validates the Adapter's chosen target with `in_reach`, so the float is presentation-only and cannot desync. The mouse has no pick-under-cursor path: moving the cursor moves the aim, and the aim weights the score.
**Example:** Facing E, aim at 10° above east. Hatch A at `r = (60, −10)`: `d = 60.8`, `θ_f = 9.5°`, `θ_a = 0.5°` → candidate; `score = 0.845 + 0.007 = 0.85`. Console B at `r = (30, 30)` (below-right): `d = 42.4`, `θ_f = 45°` (in arc), `θ_a = 55°` → `score = 0.59 + 0.79 = 1.38`. A is focused despite being farther. Crate C at `r = (−20, 40)`: `D = −20 × 256 < 0` → never a candidate. If the aim swings down to −40° (toward B): B's `θ_a = 5°` → `0.59 + 0.07 = 0.66`; A's `θ_a = 49.5°` → `0.845 + 0.71 = 1.55`; B's advantage `0.89 > 0.10` → focus switches to B.

**D.10 Hold-to-confirm bar and tap-to-confirm**

The `confirm_bar` formula (Adapter-local, render time) is defined as:

`Δt_eff = 0 if kernel PAUSED else min(Δt, CONFIRM_DT_MAX_S);  b ← clamp(b + Δt_eff / CONFIRM_HOLD_S, 0, 1) while held;  b ← max(b − CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE × Δt_eff / CONFIRM_HOLD_S, 0) while released;  on b ≥ 1: submit the declared command once, b ← 0, require release before re-arm` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the bar added uncapped wall time per frame, so one 1 s hitch confirmed a destructive cut; re-review: the bar **freezes** under `PAUSED` (`Δt_eff = 0`; it neither fills nor drains), so a destructive hold can never complete during a pause and be rejected `PAUSED`, and on resume the hold continues only if the button is still down — the PC-2 resync submits the cancel otherwise)*

Derived (target latch): while `b > 0` the bar belongs to the focus `o_latched` captured when `b` left zero; a focus change does not move or complete it (it drains at 2× on `o_latched`, then the new focus starts from 0); completion submits for `o_latched` only
Derived (`give_up` surface) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the bar for `give_up` is drawn by HUD's DOWNED-only confirm plate (UI Requirements 1) from `confirm_view(seat)`; the plate exists for the whole time `loco_state == DOWNED` (published as `confirm_view.give_up_live`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, and the bar arms only from a press edge observed while DOWNED (Edge Cases); the bar starts accumulating on the frame **after** the press edge, so with one rendered frame per tick the 0.8 s default fills 48 frames after the edge
Derived (per-verb hold; Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D80 and D81; X10 is ruled) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: in the formula above read `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` as the verb's effective hold `H_v = min(m_v × confirm_hold_s, cap_v)` (`cap_v` ≤ `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S`, below), where `confirm_hold_s` is the player's adjustable base hold (default `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` = 0.8 s), `m_v` is the multiplier the verb's owner declares (1 for every verb except the Chart Table's slot Delete, which declares 2, so Delete holds **1.6 s** at the default), and `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S` = 1.9 s is the ceiling that keeps invariant 7 true for every verb (derivation below). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-19 / C-10)* The formula is `H_v = min(m_v × confirm_hold_s, cap_v)`, where `m_v` and `cap_v` are declared by the verb's owner; `cap_v` defaults to `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S` and may never exceed it. The Chart Table declares `cap_v = CT_HOLD_CAP_S = HOLD_EFF_MAX_S − DELETE_MARGIN_MIN_S = 1.9 − 0.4 = 1.5 s` for its quit-without-saving (UI-3) and Retire (UI-5) holds (`m_v` 1), and `cap_v = HOLD_EFF_MAX_S` for slot Delete (`m_v` 2), so Delete exceeds every other Chart Table hold by at least 0.4 s at every base hold; the Settings range of `confirm_hold_s` (0.6–1.9 s, user ruling X10) is not reduced. A player who cannot hold at all uses `confirm_mode = TAP` (two taps within `CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW`), which is selectable in Settings at MVP and not only in the one-handed preset
Derived (tap mode): tap 1 on focus `o` → `armed(o)` until `t_arm + CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW`, where `t_arm` and the window advance only while the kernel is not `PAUSED` (the timer is frozen under pause); tap 2 within the window on the same `o` → submit; focus change, context change or expiry → disarmed

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| Δt | float | render frame s | calculated (the frame's `render_delta`, clamped by the presentation driver, ADR-0009) | Frame delta — real time, never a `Time.*` read (ADR-0010 SIM-02); the sim never sees the bar *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)* |
| CONFIRM_HOLD_S (registry: `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`) | float | 0.6–1.9 (**0.8**) render s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: was 0.6–1.2)* | data file; the player-adjustable Settings value `confirm_hold_s` starts here | Hold needed to confirm a destructive verb (base hold, before the verb multiplier) |
| m_v *(user rulings)* | int | {1, 2} (**1**; slot Delete **2**) | declared by the verb's owner (Interaction; Chart Table for Delete) | Per-verb multiple of the base hold; the owner also declares `cap_v` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-19 / C-10)* |
| cap_v | float | ≤ `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S` (1.9 default); Chart Table non-Delete holds 1.5 | declared by the verb's owner | Per-verb ceiling on the effective hold *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-19 / C-10)* |
| HOLD_EFF_MAX_S *(user rulings)* | float | 1.9 render s (derived) | calculated: `floor(10 × (max_age_ticks − 1) / TICK_RATE) / 10` = `floor(10 × 119 / 60) / 10` = 1.9 | Cap on any effective hold `H_v`; keeps `H_v × TICK_RATE = 114 < 120 = max_age_ticks(actions)` (invariant 7) at the largest base hold and the largest multiplier |
| CONFIRM_DT_MAX_S *(design review)* | float | 0.034–0.1 (**0.05**) render s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: lower bound 1 / `MIN_TARGET_FPS` = 1/30, invariant 17)* | data file | Cap on the frame delta the bar may use; at 20 fps and above the bar is unaffected, a 1 s hitch adds 0.05 s (6 % of the hold) |
| CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE | float | 1.5–3 (**2.0**) | data file | Drain speed as a multiple of fill speed |
| b | float | 0–1 | calculated | Bar fill; drawn on the art bible 7.3 linear bar |
| CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW | float | 2–5 (**3**) render s | data file | Arm-to-confirm window in tap mode |
| confirm_mode | enum | {HOLD, TAP} (**HOLD**; one-handed preset **TAP**; selectable on its own at MVP) | Settings | The two-tap alternative to the hold *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X10)* |

**Output Range:** `b` is clamped [0, 1]. Full drain from 1 takes `CONFIRM_HOLD_S / CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` = 0.4 s; a release-and-re-press inside that window resumes from the drained value, so a nervous thumb is forgiven but a partial hold never completes by itself. Exactly one command per completion; the bar never auto-repeats while held (release is required to re-arm). The sim sees nothing until completion — one command, immediately pre-played — so **no window length can age a command**; the Section G invariant `H_v × TICK_RATE < max_age_ticks(actions)` (48 < 120 at the default; 96 for Delete at 1.6 s; 114 at the 1.9 s cap; 90 for a Chart Table quit at the 1.5 s cap *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-19 / C-10)*) is asserted only to keep a future two-command (arm/confirm) variant safe, and `HOLD_EFF_MAX_S` is what makes it hold for every base hold the player may set and every multiplier *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. At 60 fps the fill is 48 frames; at 30 fps, 24 frames of the same 0.8 s. A frame longer than 0.05 s (below 20 fps) fills only 0.05 s of the bar, so a hold at 10 fps takes longer than 0.8 s of wall time (honest: a hitching game does not confirm a destructive verb on one slow frame); a single 1 s frame advances `b` by 0.0625 and never submits.
**Example:** Hold for 0.5 s → `b = 0.625`; release 0.1 s → `b = 0.625 − 0.25 = 0.375`; re-press and hold 0.5 s → `b = 1.0` at 0.5 s → `cut_hatch {target}` submitted, bar resets, the pre-play starts. Per-verb hold *(user rulings)*: a slot Delete (`m_v = 2`) at the default base hold needs `H_v = min(2 × 0.8, 1.9) = 1.6 s` (96 ticks); with the player's base hold set to 1.2 s it needs `min(2.4, 1.9) = 1.9 s`, never more; a plain cut at 1.2 s needs 1.2 s. **Worked check (per-verb cap)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-19 / C-10)*: base 0.6: quit 0.6, Delete 1.2, margin 0.6; base 0.8: 0.8, 1.6, 0.8; base 0.95: 0.95, 1.9, 0.95; base 1.2: 1.2, 1.9, 0.7; base 1.5: 1.5, 1.9, 0.4; base 1.9: quit `min(1.9, 1.5)` = 1.5, Delete `min(3.8, 1.9)` = 1.9, margin 0.4. The minimum margin over the whole range is 0.4 s (reached from a base of 1.5 s up); compare in integer microseconds (1,900,000 − 1,500,000 = 400,000 ≥ 400,000) because 1.9 − 1.5 is not exact in floating point. At base 1.9 and 60 fps (dt 16,667 µs): quit / Retire fires on frame 90 (89 × 16,667 = 1,483,363 < 1,500,000; 90 × 16,667 = 1,500,030) and Delete on frame 114 (113 × 16,667 = 1,883,371; 114 × 16,667 = 1,900,038). Tap mode: tap at `t = 10.0 s` arms the hatch; tap at `t = 12.9 s` confirms; a tap at `t = 13.1 s` would have found the arm expired and would re-arm instead.

**D.11 Distance-driven animation — odometer, stride, frame index, reversed cycle, time-stepped states**

The `frame_index` formula (Presenter's, from Body's `odometer`) is defined as:

`frame_moving = posmod(anim_counter, 8)`, where `anim_counter += dir × (cycle_pos − cycle_pos_prev)` and `cycle_pos = floor(odometer / (STRIDE[gait] × 256))`

Derived (Body, sim state, per tick, post-collision): `odometer += (|moved_x| + |moved_y|) × ODO_W[octant]`; `ODO_W = 256` cardinal, `181` diagonal (1/256 px)
Derived: `dir = +1` forward, `−1` when `dot(UNIT[octant], UNIT[facing]) < 0` (back-pedal)
Derived (looping time-stepped states — work 6, carry-idle = carry frame 0): `frame_timed = posmod(floor((T − T_enter) × ANIM_FPS / TICK_RATE), n)`
Derived (idle breathing, tick-stepped) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; free-running from the committed tick, ADR-0019 § 7.1; Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0019 sync)*: `frame_idle = posmod(floor(T / IDLE_FRAME_TICKS), 6)` with `IDLE_FRAME_TICKS` = 24 (a 144-tick breath, ≈ 0.42 Hz, the "~0.5 Hz" of the art direction); it is derived from the sim tick `T`, not from `present_time`, because the replay criterion requires byte-identical per-tick Presenter samples for a recorded input trace (Two-rhythm invariance; per-frame placements vary with alpha by design) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync)* — it also freezes under `PAUSED` because `T` does
Derived (one-shot states — stagger 4, down 6): `frame_timed = min(n − 1, floor((T − T_enter) × ANIM_FPS / TICK_RATE))`
Derived: `anim_fps_moving = v × TICK_RATE / STRIDE[gait]`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| moved_x, moved_y | int | −3–3 | calculated (D.3, after collision) | Pixels actually moved on each axis this tick |
| ODO_W[o] | int | {181, 256} | constant | Per-pixel distance weight so a diagonal (1, 1) step counts 1.414 px, not 2 or 1 |
| odometer | int (64-bit) | 0–unbounded, monotonic | calculated (`body.state.odometer`, persisted) | Post-collision distance in 1/256 px |
| STRIDE[gait] | int | ±2 around **10 / 15 / 5** (walk / run / carry) base px | data file (`assets/data/animation`) | Pixels per animation frame |
| cycle_pos | int | monotonic | calculated | Whole frames of travel so far |
| dir | int | {+1, −1} | calculated (Presenter, from committed `octant` and `facing`) | Cycle direction; reversed when back-pedalling |
| anim_counter | int (64-bit) | unbounded, signed | calculated (Presenter-local; one per drawn body) | Continuous cycle phase *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; `7 − frame` at a sign change would jump the cycle; a counter that merely changes sign of increment keeps the frame continuous)* |
| ANIM_FPS | int | **12** | constant (art bible 5.x) | Time-stepped animation rate (work, stagger, down; not idle) |
| IDLE_FRAME_TICKS *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int | 12–36 (**24**) | data file | Ticks per idle-breathing frame; the idle cycle is `6 × this` ticks |
| n | int | 4–8 | data file (sheet frame counts) | Frames in the current sheet |
| frame_moving, frame_timed | int | 0–n−1 | calculated | Index the Presenter writes |

**Output Range:** `odometer` is 64-bit: at 768 units/tick (run) it reaches 2^63 after ≈ 1.9 × 10^14 s — overflow is impossible and unhandled beyond a width assertion (kernel D.14 pattern). With `STRIDE = v × TICK_RATE / ANIM_FPS = 5v` the moving cycle runs at **exactly 12 fps on every gait and in every octant**: cardinal walk adds 512/tick → a frame every 5 ticks; diagonal walk adds `(k + k) × 181` per tick, averaging 511.9/tick (28 units short per 256 ticks — one dropped frame (2560 units) every ≈ 23,400 ticks ≈ 6.5 min of continuous diagonal walking, invisible). Sliding along a wall on a diagonal (one axis blocked) animates at **0.50** of the cardinal pace (`ODO_W` 181 per pixel on one axis: 1.414 px/tick × 181 = 256 units against 512 for a cardinal), while the body itself slides at 0.707 of its speed, so the cycle runs slower than the feet's ground speed during a slide *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text claimed 0.707 here and in Edge Cases and an acceptance criterion, while another criterion fixed the weight at 181, which gives 0.50; the two contradicted each other)*. The rule is **Ship Body's (BDY-19, `ODO_W[move octant]`)** and this GDD does not contradict it: it documents the 0.50 consequence. If the playtest finds the slide cycle too slow, the alternative that restores 0.707 is to weight by the axes that actually moved (256 per pixel for a single-axis move, 181 per pixel for a two-axis move); that is Body's change, recorded as Open Question 22. Walking into a wall adds 0 and the cycle freezes on its current frame (PC-18 test 8). `frame_moving` is continuous across octant changes, facing changes, gait changes (the stride divisor changes but `cycle_pos_prev` is re-based on the same tick, so no jump) and the forward/reverse switch. One-shot states clamp on the last frame: the 4-frame stagger finishes at tick 20 and holds for the remaining `STAGGER_TICKS − 20`; the 6-frame down finishes at tick 30 — so **`STAGGER_TICKS ≥ 20` is a floor** (the quantities block's 18 would truncate the sheet; Section G corrects the range to 20–45). Remote bodies use the same formulas from MIRROR state; `anim_counter` is per-Presenter and may differ between clients by a whole cycle without any pillar consequence (phase, not pose, is unreplicated).
**Example:** Walking east, `odometer` goes 0, 512, 1024 … → `cycle_pos` 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1 (tick 5) … → frame 1 at tick 5, frame 2 at tick 10, wraps at tick 40: 12 fps. Back-pedal: walking W while facing E (`dot < 0`) with `anim_counter = 5` → frames 5, 4, 3 … ; the player turns to face W mid-stride (`dot > 0` now) at `anim_counter = 3` → frames 3, 4, 5 …; nothing jumps. Stagger entered at T = 5000: frames 0, 1, 2, 3 at T = 5000, 5005, 5010, 5015; frame 3 held through T = 5029; IDLE at T = 5030.

**D.12 Chest point (beam origin) and hand point (tool placement)**

*User decision 2026-10-26: the lamp is chest-mounted and hands-free (Pandorum); the beam's origin is the chest point. `HAND_POINT` survives only to place tool sprites and carries no light.*

The `chest_point` formula (Presenter for the drawn light; `ControllerView` getters at read time for the sim value, using `pos_curr`; `controller.step` stores nothing, as it runs before `body.integrate` and a stored value would be one tick stale) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix, ADR-0019 § 2.2)* is defined as:

`chest = floor(lerp(pos_prev, pos_curr, tick_alpha)) + CHEST_POINT[sheet][anim] ⊙ (flip_h ? (−1, 1) : (1, 1))`

Derived (sim value, no `tick_alpha` — H9): chest point `= pos_curr + CHEST_POINT[sheet][anim] ⊙ mirror`, computed at read time by `ControllerView.get_chest_x` / `get_chest_y`, never stored *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*
Derived (Presenter only, tool sprite anchor, no light): `hand = floor(lerp(pos_prev, pos_curr, tick_alpha)) + HAND_POINT[sheet][anim] ⊙ (flip_h ? (−1, 1) : (1, 1))`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| pos_prev, pos_curr | vec2 int | — | calculated (`body.state`) | Committed foot positions |
| tick_alpha | float | 0–1 | registry | Interpolation fraction (clamped) |
| CHEST_POINT[sheet][anim] | vec2 int | **`|x| ≤ 2`**, `y` in −45–−25 (chest height on a 53-px crewmate) | data file (from the five drawn sheets, per animation row: idle, walk, run, carry, work, stagger, down) | Beam origin offset from the floored foot position; near the centreline by construction (a chest lamp sits on the pack's front plate) |
| HAND_POINT[sheet][anim] | vec2 int | sheet-sane (`|x| ≤ 12`, `y` in −40–−15) | data file | Tool sprite anchor; **no light**; the tool changes hands on a mirror (accepted, 5.6) |
| flip_h | bool | — | calculated (D.8) | Mirrored facing |
| chest | vec2 int | — | calculated | Light origin, same floored position as the sprite (PR-4 rule 2) |
| hand | vec2 int | — | calculated | Tool sprite anchor |

**Output Range:** Integer base px; the light and the sprite share one `floor`, so they can never disagree by a pixel. A mirror flip moves the lamp by exactly `2|x| ≤ 4` px — the lint bound **`|x| ≤ 2`** is a load-time assertion on the data file (Section E; it replaces the earlier `|CHEST_POINT.x| ≤ 2` (chest lamp; hand point lightless) hand-swap concern, which no longer applies to the light). The per-animation row changes the point by whatever the art says (the carry posture and the run lean tilt the plate; stagger and down move it) — these are the *only* permitted "shakes" of the beam: its origin. Aim angle, cone, reach and temperature are never functions of this value. Because the lamp is hands-free, **the beam is available while carrying and while channelling** (PC-8's open question resolves as *available*; the two-handed work loop does not touch it); the carry and work rows must keep the lamp unobstructed — an art lint (Section E). The sim-side value uses `pos_curr` and is what `emitter.place` and the Perception Filter's horror-rig substitution read; the drawn value leads it by at most one tick of interpolation. `HAND_POINT` has no sim consumer.
**Example:** `pos_prev = (300, 200)`, `pos_curr = (302, 200)`, `tick_alpha = 0.42` → `floor((300.84, 200)) = (300, 200)`; facing W (`sheet = E`, `flip_h`), walk row `CHEST_POINT[E][walk] = (1, −34)` → `chest = (300 − 1, 200 − 34) = (299, 166)`. Turning to face E on the next tick: `chest = (302 + 1, 166) = (303, 166)` — a 2-px mirror jump plus the 2-px step. The tool sprite at `HAND_POINT[E][walk] = (7, −28)` mirrored → `(293, 172)`, swapping to `(309, 172)` on the turn — visible, lightless, accepted.

**D.13 Noise stimulus — per tick, per tile, per second**

The `noise_tick` formula (hook `controller.noise`, divisor 1, `after: [body.integrate]` — a second Controller hook, because `controller.step` runs before Body and cannot see this tick's motion (Ship Body CP-3, option b); on each tick with `noise_T > 0` it calls `push_stimulus(NOISE, actor, class)` with `class` = WALK, RUN or CARRY from `loco_state`, and Attention's intake maps the class to its magnitude) is defined as: *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics, attention-system)*

`noise_T = (moved_x, moved_y) ≠ (0, 0) ? NOISE[loco_state] : 0`, where `NOISE` is **total** over `loco_state`: `NOISE[WALK] = 1`, `NOISE[RUN] = 4`, `NOISE[CARRY_WALK] = 1`, and 0 for every other state (`IDLE`, `CHANNELLING`, `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD`), so displacement that is not locomotion (a pod hop's body transfer, a shove) pushes no gait stimulus *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; with `(moved_x, moved_y) = moved(actor) := pos_curr − pos_prev` (Body's derived read, valid only from a hook after `body.integrate`)

`burden_T = (carry_ref != none AND salvage_carry AND body_of_actor(actor) != HOME_BODY AND loco_state ∉ {IN_POD, DOWNED, DEAD}) ? U_BURDEN : 0`, pushed as class `BURDEN` from the same hook on every tick it is non-zero, moving or not; the moved-only gate of `noise_T` applies to the gait classes only (PC-10) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)*

Derived: `noise_per_tile = NOISE[g] × TILE_PX / v_g`
Derived: `noise_per_s = NOISE[g] × TICK_RATE`
Derived: `ratio_tile(run : walk) = (NOISE_RUN / NOISE_WALK) × (v_walk / v_run)`
Derived: burden per Attention run (6 ticks) = 6 × `U_BURDEN` × 1000 mAU = 42,000 at 7, plus the CARRY gait push 6 × 1 × 1000 = 6,000 on a moving run = 48,000 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| NOISE[g] (registry: `NOISE_PER_TICK`) | int | ratio-preserving (**WALK 1, RUN 4, CARRY_WALK 1**; 0 for every non-locomotion state *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) | data file (Attention units, 1 AU = one walking tick; this GDD owns the gait table and Attention's intake maps each `push_stimulus` class to it; the 4 : 1 ratio is the design) | Stimulus per moving tick by locomotion state (RUN includes its ramp ticks: the lean is already playing) |
| moved_x, moved_y | int | — | calculated (D.3; Body's derived `moved(actor)`, read in `controller.noise`) | Post-collision motion; a body pushing a wall makes no footsteps |
| v_g | int | 1–3 | data file | Gait speed |
| noise_T | int | {0, 1, 4} | calculated | Derived magnitude of this tick's push (the push itself carries the class WALK / RUN / CARRY) |
| noise_per_tile | int | 24 / 64 / 48 | calculated | Walk / run / carry |
| noise_per_s | int | 60 / 240 / 60 | calculated | Walk / run / carry while moving |
| ratio_tile | float | 2.67 | calculated | Running's per-tile cost relative to walking |
| U_BURDEN | int | 7 AU [PLACEHOLDER] (registry, Attention-owned; cited, not defined here) | registry (`carry_burden_intake`, Attention F-26) | Magnitude of the per-tick BURDEN push while a salvage carry is held aboard a non-home body *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)* |
| salvage_carry | bool | — | `controller.state` (PC-8) | True while the carried item was picked up aboard a non-home body *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)* |
| burden_T | int | {0, `U_BURDEN`} | calculated | This tick's BURDEN push *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)* |

**Output Range:** Per tick 0, 1 or 4; Attention (divisor 6) sees the sum of up to six ticks, 0–24 per run of gait noise; a held salvage carry adds 6 × `U_BURDEN` = 42 AU per run on top, moving or not *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)*. Running is 4× louder per second and **2.67× louder per tile** (`4 × 2/3`) — the Section G invariant `ratio_tile > 1` is what makes running a real trade rather than a free retreat; it would fail if `NOISE_RUN / NOISE_WALK ≤ v_run / v_walk = 1.5`. Carrying is as loud per second as walking but **2× louder per tile** (48 vs 24) because it is slower — an intended consequence (extraction under rising Attention is the sortie's tension), recorded here so a balance pass does not read it as an error. Gait noise is a function of locomotion state and motion only, and the BURDEN push of the carry state and the holder's body only *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP; user ruling X3, delegated ruling R5)*; nothing in either reads Attention, rhythm or sanity.
**Example:** A 10-tile corridor: walked in 240 ticks → 240 noise; run in 160 ticks (+ 6 ramp ticks at 4) → ≈ 640 noise; carried in 480 ticks → 480 noise. Walking into a wall for 60 ticks → 0.

**D.14 Timers — stagger, downed-to-death, run latch, device switch, name reveal**

The `death_tick` formula (the downed-to-death timer, with its normative check order) is defined as:

`death_tick = T_downed + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`, lowered by an applied `give_up` to `death_tick := min(death_tick, T_apply)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D7)*; each tick while DOWNED: `if cap ≠ NONE → leave DOWNED for the class member (States step 4)  else if T ≥ death_tick → DEAD` (cap checked first, steps 1–2 of the `controller.step` order) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

Derived: `stagger_end_tick = T_stagger + STAGGER_TICKS` (input ignored until then; channel cleared; no displacement)
Derived: `latch_clear_tick = T_stop + RUN_LATCH_CLEAR` (toggle mode; also cleared on channel start and non-`gameplay` context entry)
Derived (Adapter, injected monotonic `Clock.now_us()`, not `Time.*`): `device_switch_ok = (t_now − t_last_switch) ≥ DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` (glyph set follows the last button/key device when true) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*
Derived (Presenter, injected monotonic `Clock.now_us()`, not `Time.*`, VS-B, lit targets only): `name_reveal_end = t_press + NAME_REVEAL_S` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| STAGGER_TICKS | int | 20–45 (**30**) | data file | Stagger duration; floor 20 = 4 frames at 12 fps (D.11) |
| DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS | int | 600–3600 (**1800** = 30 s) | data file | Solo recovery window before the death stub, and the VS-B revive window; the solo player may end it early with `give_up` (user ruling D7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| T_apply, GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS *(user rulings; re-review)* | int tick; int | — ; **3** = the minimum over **all** confirm modes: TAP mode is `3` (the first tap is sampled no earlier than `T_downed + 1`, a release frame follows, the second tap no earlier than `T_downed + 3`, so the two taps are at least 2 ticks apart and `give_up` applies in P2 of tick `T_downed + 3`); HOLD mode is `ceil(H_give_up × TICK_RATE)` = 36 at the lowest base hold (0.6 s) and 48 at the 0.8 s default (derived figures, not floors) | calculated | Tick at which `give_up` applies; the shortest `T_dead − T_downed` a give-up can produce over every mode and every legal base hold, so Save/Load's loss floor (`loss_min = GIVE_UP_MIN_TICKS + 1` = 4) is a true lower bound; the earlier 48 held only for the default hold and was not a floor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: registry value 48 → 3)* |
| RUN_LATCH_CLEAR | int | 15–60 (**30**) | data file | Ticks of `octant == NONE` before a toggled run clears |
| DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS | int | 100–500 (**250**) ms | data file | Minimum real time (injected `Clock`) between glyph-set switches |
| NAME_REVEAL_S | float | 1–3 (**2**) render s | data file | Press-to-reveal crewmate name duration |
| T_stagger, T_downed, T_stop | int | — | calculated (`controller.state`) | Entry ticks |
| death_tick, stagger_end_tick, latch_clear_tick | int | — | calculated | Expiry ticks |

**Output Range:** All sim timers are integer ticks and freeze under `PAUSED` (T does not advance); the two real-time ones (device switch, name reveal) are Adapter/Presenter-local, read the injected monotonic `Clock` and never a `Time.*` call, and never touch the sim *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*. The downed check order is normative: at `death_tick` the cap is read first, so a recovery Suit has already written is honoured on the same tick — and because `suit.vitals` runs before `controller.step` (`after:`), a cap rise computed on `death_tick` itself wins. Suit runs at divisor 6, so a rise it would compute "between" runs is seen up to 5 ticks late; that is the tolerance PC-9 accepts. A `SYSTEM` `stagger` that is `Applied` while the actor is already `STAGGERED` does not restart or extend the timer; `stagger_end_tick` keeps its original value, so a stagger never chains into a stun-lock. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)*
**Example (give up)** *(user rulings; re-review: Suit ticks are multiples of 6 and the frame/tick fixture is stated)*: one rendered frame per tick; Suit runs at multiples of 6, so downed at T = 10,002 with the cap staying `NONE`; the player's press edge is observed at T = 10,104 and the bar fills 48 frames later, at T = 10,152 (0.8 s = 48 ticks) → `give_up` applies in P2 of tick 10,152, `death_tick := min(11,802, 10,152) = 10,152`, `controller.step` reads cap `NONE` and `T ≥ death_tick` → `DEAD` on tick 10,152, one `request_restore`; had Suit's run at T = 10,152 (a multiple of 6) written `cap = WALK_ONLY`, the cap-first check would give the class member (`IDLE` with no direction held) and the give-up is spent harmlessly. 
**Example:** Downed at T = 10,000 → `death_tick = 11,800`. Suit's run at T = 11,796 (a multiple of 6) computes `cap = WALK_ONLY` → at T = 11,797 the controller reads it → the class member, `IDLE` with no direction held (stagger does not fire on a *rising* edge). If instead the rise would have been computed at T = 11,801, the controller at T = 11,800 reads `NONE` → DEAD. Toggle-run: stop at T = 3000 → latch clears at T = 3030 unless movement resumes before then.

**D.15 Input-to-first-pixel latency**

The `L_pixel` formula (this GDD's instance of kernel D.4, with the Adapter's sampling point fixed) is defined as:

`L_pixel = L_sample + L_tick + L_interp + L_render`

Derived: `L_sample ∈ [0, frame_duration)` — age of the device event at the Adapter's `_process` sample
Derived: `L_tick ≈ 0` wall — sample → `Kernel.step()` is the same call; one tick of *simulated* latency by construction (P1 freeze → P2 apply → P3 integrate)
Derived: `L_render ∈ [0, frame_duration]` — P5 notify → the frame that draws it
Derived *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `L_interp = tick_duration / step_px` — the Presenter draws `floor(lerp(pos_prev, pos_curr, tick_alpha))`, so a tick's motion is spread over the following render interval and the first whole pixel appears only when `tick_alpha × step_px ≥ 1`, i.e. after `1 / step_px` of a tick (8.3 ms at the 2 px walk step, 5.6 ms at the 3 px run step, up to 16.7 ms at a 1 px step); a sub-pixel position is never drawn (PR-4), so the earlier "first visible sub-step that frame" does not exist

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| frame_duration | float | 16.7 ms at 60 fps; 33.3 at 30 | calculated (engine) | Render period |
| L_sample | float | 0–16.7 ms | calculated | Wall age of the newest input event when the Adapter samples immediately before `Kernel.step()` |
| L_tick | float | ≈ 0 ms wall (exactly 1 simulated tick) | calculated | Sample-to-commit within one `Kernel.step()` call |
| L_render | float | 0–16.7 ms | calculated | Same-frame draw: the Adapter and the TickDriver precede the presentation nodes in one `_process` pass by process priority (ADR-0001 § 2; spike KRN-LAT-02 verifies) |
| L_interp *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | float | 5.6–16.7 ms | calculated | Delay from the tick's commit to the first whole-pixel step of the lerp-then-floor draw: `tick_duration / step_px` |
| L_pixel | float | ≈ 6–42 ms at the 2 px walk step (≈ 6–39 at the 3 px run step, 6–50 at a 1 px step; + display scan-out) | calculated | Press → the body's first moved pixel |
| L_ack | float | ≤ frame_duration | calculated | Press → pre-play (sound, hand animation, bar): 0 ticks by construction |

**Output Range:** Best case a few ms plus `L_interp` (event arrives just before the sample, tick runs, same frame draws, first pixel after `L_interp`); worst ≈ 33 ms + `L_interp` at 60 fps, i.e. ≈ 42 ms for the 2 px walk step (event just after a sample waits a frame, then draws next frame, then waits for the first whole pixel) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the sim contribution is exactly one tick and never two, because the sample precedes P0 of the tick it lands in (submit before `step()` → `queued_tick = T − 1`, in tick T's batch). The pre-play acknowledgement is independent of all of this and lands in the same frame as the press. With a full queue add `floor(q_pos / 128) × 16.7 ms` (kernel D.4) — unreachable by a single local issuer sending ≤ 2 intents + taps per frame. Anything slower than 33 ms + `L_interp` at 60 fps is a bug in the sampling point, not a tuning matter.
**Example:** Key down at 3 ms into frame k; sample at 4 ms; tick k: P2 applies `set_move_intent`, P3 moves 2 px; P5 at 6 ms; `_process` at 7 ms places the sprite with `tick_alpha` ≈ 0.2, where `floor(lerp)` is still the old pixel (no sub-pixel is ever drawn); the first whole pixel needs `tick_alpha ≥ 0.5` at the 2 px step (`L_interp` ≈ 8.3 ms after the commit) and is drawn on a following frame: `L_pixel ≈ 14–40 ms` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `L_interp` added)*. Key down at 5 ms, just after the sample → tick k+1 at ≈ 20.7 ms, drawn ≈ 33 ms, plus `L_interp` ≈ 8 ms for the first whole pixel.

**D.16 Glowstick throw — binding and arc placeholders (Interaction / Suit & Equipment own the mechanics)**

*User decision 2026-10-26: throwable glowsticks are Suit & Equipment's deployable lights — thrown on a short arc, landing as an omnidirectional world light. The controller binds the action and hands over the aim; the quantities are placeholders owned by Interaction.*

The `throw_glowstick` formula is defined as:

`land = foot + dir(aim_angle) × THROW_RANGE_TILES × TILE_PX`, clipped by Interaction at the first wall footprint along the path

Derived (the command the Adapter submits on a `throw` press; coalesce NONE, actor OWNED_BY_ISSUER, actor_source body.state): `throw_glowstick { aim_angle: aim_q }` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
Derived: `land_tick = apply_tick + THROW_ARC_TICKS`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| `throw` | action | KB **Q**, pad **RB** (R3 retired 2026-10-27 — design review; ux-designer owns the binding) | data file (binding table) | A **separate action**, not a hold on `carry_drop` *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; a tap/hold split on `G` would delay the drop's acknowledgement by the hold threshold and reuse the hold grammar that means "confirm a destructive verb")* |
| aim_q | int | 0–4095 | calculated (D.7) | The throw goes where the lamp points — no separate throw aim |
| foot | vec2 int | — | calculated (`body.state.pos`) | Foot origin |
| THROW_RANGE_TILES | float | 1–5 (**3**) | data file (Interaction's) | Landing distance along the aim, placeholder |
| THROW_ARC_TICKS | int | 15–60 (**30** = 0.5 s) | data file (Interaction's) | Flight time, placeholder; the light exists only on landing |
| land | vec2 int | — | calculated (Interaction) | Landing point, floored, after wall clipping |
| land_tick | int | — | calculated (Interaction) | Tick the world light appears |

**Output Range:** Placeholders; the controller asserts nothing about them beyond integer ticks. Preconditions (Interaction's `validate`): not carrying (hands full → `BUSY`), not channelling (`BUSY`), not staggered/downed, a glowstick available (`OUT_OF_CHARGES`, immediate class). Pre-play: the throwing-arm animation and the throw sound; the glowstick object and its light appear only on committed state (a light is a Pillar 1 anchor and is never predicted — the same rule as the beam toggle, PC-5). The throw does not change locomotion state and never locks movement.
**Example:** Facing and aiming E, `THROW_RANGE_TILES = 3`: `land = foot + (144, 0)`; a wall footprint at +100 px clips it to the last free pixel before the wall; the light lands 30 ticks after apply.

## Edge Cases

Every outcome below is the exact behaviour of the Adapter (presentation side), the Controller Sim or Body's `LocomotionStep`; "diagnostics" names the counter or record the Debug Overlay exposes (`PC_*`, following the kernel's `WRITE_OUTSIDE_SCOPE` style; Adapter-side counters are presentation diagnostics, sim-side ones live in `controller.step`'s diagnostics channel, never in a block). The `PC_*` names below are frozen by this GDD under the Debug Overlay's channel convention (registry `diag_panel_contract`: the owner's prefix, units in the declaration, not the name); `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` is Ship Body's counter (registry), active in release. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values, ship-body-and-interior-physics)* Items marked *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26)* fill a gap in Section C and are recommendations the orchestrator adopts unless they reopen a user decision; the owner named "to confirm" ratifies the mechanism.

**Movement input**

- **If `move_left` and `move_right` (A + D) are held together**: the keyboard vector composes to `s.x = 0` (D.6); with no vertical key the octant is NONE and the body stands; with W also held the octant is N — opposite keys cancel, they do not alternate. The run flag is still carried in the intent. No diagnostics. *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; "last-pressed wins" is deterministic too but needs press-order state; cancellation is the simpler invariant and an option can add SOCD later.)*
- **If all four move keys are held**: `s = (0, 0)` → `octant = NONE`; the body is IDLE, a channel may begin, the run latch's clear timer runs. Nothing is submitted beyond the one NONE intent on the change. No diagnostics.
- **If the stick sits exactly on a sector boundary (e.g. `θ = 22.5°`)**: on entry from the deadzone `o_raw = floor((22.5 + 22.5) / 45) = 1 (NE)` — `floor` assigns a boundary to the upper sector deterministically; once in a sector the hysteresis holds it until `δ > 27.5°`, so a stick resting on the boundary never chatters. Diagnostics: `PC_OCTANT_CHANGES` per second (Adapter) — a rate above 10/s with a stationary stick indicates a deadzone or hysteresis regression.
- **If the stick is inside the movement deadzone while `run` is held**: `octant = NONE`, so `v_eff = 0` (D.4) and the state is IDLE; the intent carries `run = true`; `ramp_ticks` does not count. In toggle mode the latch is set by the press and starts its 30-tick clear timer immediately because `octant == NONE`; if the stick leaves the deadzone before tick 30 the run begins with its ramp. No "run in place" animation exists — the idle sheet plays. No diagnostics.
- **If run is toggled on and the player then stops**: at `T_stop + RUN_LATCH_CLEAR` (30 ticks = 0.5 s) the Adapter clears the latch and sends `set_move_intent {NONE, run: false}` (a change of `run` is a change). A movement input before tick 30 resumes at RUN with a fresh ramp; after it, at WALK. A latch never survives a channel start or a context change either (PC-3). Diagnostics: `PC_RUN_LATCH_AUTOCLEAR` (Adapter).
- **If `run` is pressed while carrying**: the intent carries `run = true`; `controller.step` resolves `v_eff = v_carry` (carry beats run, D.4); state stays CARRY_WALK; the pressed key changes nothing on screen — the carry-walk cycle *is* the reason, and the HUD does not banner it — but the Adapter plays the one local `cue_input_ignored` click (PC-2; Adapter-local knowledge, reveals no sim result) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: silent ignores are an accessibility gap)*. **While at Suit's CRITICAL (cap `WALK_ONLY`)**: `v_eff = v_walk`, state WALK, `run` a no-op without resetting `sub` (PC-18 test 12); the breath/warmth readings and the visor fog are the readable reason. **While channelling**: `run` alone does not change `octant`, so the channel continues; the intent's `run = true` is recorded; the moment an octant arrives the channel aborts and the run begins. In toggle mode a press during a channel sets no latch (channel start clears it, and a press during one is ignored for latching) *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; otherwise releasing the channel would launch an unasked-for run)*. Diagnostics: `PC_RUN_SUPPRESSED{carry|cap|channel}` (sim).
- **If the body moves diagonally into a wall corner with x blocked and y free**: the x-sweep stops at the footprint edge (`moved_x = 0`), the y-sweep proceeds at the diagonal per-axis rate (181/256 × `v_eff`), so the body slides along the wall at 0.707 of its speed; `odometer` advances by `|moved_y| × 181` (Body's BDY-19) and the walk cycle plays at **0.50** pace while the feet slide at 0.707 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: corrected from 0.707; see D.11 and Open Question 22)*; noise is pushed by `controller.noise` (it moved). `sub.x` keeps accumulating and truncating — it never grows beyond 255 because `step_x` is requested and refused each tick; no energy is stored. **Both axes blocked** (a concave corner, or a 1-px gap narrower than the 16-px foot box): `moved = (0, 0)`, `odometer` unchanged, the cycle freezes on its frame, **no noise** this tick (`controller.noise` reads `moved == (0, 0)`, D.13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics)*, the state remains WALK/RUN (the player is asking to move; the world is refusing); the run ramp still counts. Diagnostics: `PC_BLOCKED_TICKS` (sim, per actor) — a high steady count with a non-NONE intent flags a layout snag.
- **If the body walks into a door whose leaf is closing**: at MVP a leaf's footprint is binary — projected as solid on the tick its state becomes `CLOSED` (PC-18 test 7) and absent while `OPEN`/`CLOSING` — so the body passes until that tick and stops at the leaf's edge from it onward; the closing animation is presentation. If Layout later projects intermediate footprints for a closing leaf, the same sweep handles them with no controller change. Diagnostics: none (ordinary collision).
- **If a door is commanded to close while a foot box overlaps its leaf footprint**: **`PLAYER` origin** — Interaction's `validate` must fail with the reject token `OBSTRUCTED` (immediate class: ack withdrawn within a frame, the dull "no" cue); the leaf stays open. **`SYSTEM` origin** (a failure cascade slamming a pressure door) — the door's owner enters a `CLOSING_BLOCKED` state and retries the close on each of its hook runs, completing it on the first run where no foot box overlaps; **the body is never displaced** (an uncaused motion the player did not make — C-R extended to the body) and a footprint rectangle may never be enabled while it overlaps an actor's foot box. Body's sweep (BDY-34) asserts in debug when it meets such an overlap; in debug and release alike Body's sweep admits only sub-steps that do not deepen the overlap with each exempt rectangle (registry `overlap_exemption`; the body is still never displaced, and `pos` is unchanged on every tick the actor does not step), and increments the counter. *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; Layout and Interaction to confirm the reason code and state name.)* Diagnostics: `PC_DOOR_CLOSE_BLOCKED` (sim), `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` (Ship Body's counter, registry `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP`: in the `body.integrate` diagnostics channel, active in release as well as debug — should read 0 in every clean build). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system, ship-body-and-interior-physics, ship-section-and-layout-data-model)*
- **If the actor stands on a section seam when the section detaches**: Body assigns the actor to the body whose section contains the **foot origin** (horizontally centred; the box's top row) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics)* on the detach tick — a foot box straddling the seam is not split; the actor transfers wholly with that side *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; Body to confirm)*. The transfer is a body change: Body sets `pos_prev := pos_curr` in the new frame and `sub = (0, 0)` (no interpolation across frames), `controller.step` clears `channel_ref`, `channel_face` and `channel_body` on that tick through step 1's body edge (the actor's body id changed; a body transfer interrupts a channel; States table) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6; INTERNAL fix)*, keeps `octant`, `carry_ref` and `facing`; the camera treats a weld/detach `BODY_TRANSFER` carrying `frame_delta_px` as a REBASE, not a cut (a pod or transit body transfer is an unconditional cut; registry `camera_cut_predicates`) because the follow target's frame changed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from pixel-perfect-render-pipeline-camera)*. The HUD's prompt, if the focused object went with the other section, disappears the same frame (it is no longer in reach). Diagnostics: `PC_BODY_TRANSFER{detach|pod}` (sim), with the seam and the side taken.

**Kernel state and resync**

- **If an intent result arrives `Rejected(STALE)`**: the Adapter reverts `last_acked` for that intent type to "unknown" and on the next sampled frame resends its current quantised value, bypassing the change filter once (PC-2 resync rule, kernel E "a coalesced intent rejected STALE"). Unreachable locally at the kernel's defaults (queue drains in 8 ticks < 30); it is the VS-B guest's path. Diagnostics: `PC_RESYNC{STALE}` (Adapter).
- **If the kernel is `PAUSED`**: the Adapter samples devices but submits **no intents and no commands** (anything that slipped through would be `Rejected(PAUSED)` immediately and never queued — kernel E); the Adapter's in-world hold timers (confirm bar, name reveal) are frozen with the pause (`Δt_eff = 0`, D.10: the confirm bar neither fills nor drains while `PAUSED`, so a destructive hold cannot complete during a pause and be rejected `PAUSED`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the Chart Table's UI-3 destructive-action bar runs on its own UI clock while `PAUSED` (CT-22) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from chart-table-web-map-and-menus)*; the channel's progress is frozen in the owner's block. On `PAUSED → RUNNING` the Adapter resends both intents unconditionally (resync trigger ii) and, for a hold-mode channel, submits `cancel_channel` if `interact` is physically up — the hold survives only if the button is still down. A control latched by PC-2a (d) because its press closed the layer resends nothing on this resync, to the layer or to gameplay, until it has been released and pressed again; `interact` pressed on Resume therefore produces no gameplay `interact` edge on the resume frame *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; answers Chart Table ASK-CT-15)*. Diagnostics: `PC_RESYNC{RESUME}`, `PC_PAUSED_SUBMIT_ATTEMPT` (Adapter — should read 0; a nonzero value means something submitted while paused).
- **If `LOADING → RUNNING` occurs with movement keys physically held**: the restored block has `octant = NONE, run = false` (PC-17); on the first RUNNING frame the Adapter samples and sends the current octant and `run` regardless of `last_acked`, so the body starts moving on the first tick (resync trigger iii — "the sim's intent equals the hands within one tick"); the run latch (toggle) is cleared by the load, so a toggled run does not resume; no channel exists after a restore (`CHANNELLING → IDLE`), so a held `interact` does nothing until released and pressed again — the pre-play token map is emptied at `LOADING`. Diagnostics: `PC_RESYNC{LOAD}` (Adapter).
- **If the window loses focus mid-channel**: the Adapter submits `cancel_channel` and `set_move_intent {NONE, run: false}`, marks every held action released, zeroes the confirm bar and clears the run latch; the channel's bar drains at 2× and the "stopped" cue plays. On focus return it resyncs (trigger iv): the channel is **not** resumed automatically even if `interact` is still physically down — a fresh press is required, because the cancel was sent and a silent restart would be a command the player did not issue. **Mid-run**: the NONE intent stops the body one tick later; on focus return the resync resends the current octant and `run`, so if the key is still held the body resumes within one tick (with a fresh ramp). Diagnostics: `PC_FOCUS_LOST{channel|moving|idle}`, `PC_RESYNC{FOCUS}` (Adapter).

**Aim**

- **If the mouse cursor is within `AIM_MOUSE_DEADZONE_PX` (8 base px) of the fixed aim origin** (`pos_curr + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)`, PC-5): raw aim holds its last value; no `set_aim` is sent; the light, the look-ahead and the focus score all use the held value. Leaving the deadzone resumes normally. The origin does not depend on facing, so a mirror flip moves nothing in the measurement (the drawn lamp moves ≤ 4 px, D.12, without turning the beam); a cursor parked on the body holds the aim until it moves. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* No diagnostics.
- **If the cursor is held still while the camera look-ahead settles** (the cursor 20, 60 or 200 px from the avatar): `cursor_aim_world` is measured against `f_q`, which the look-ahead does not move, so `aim_q` is constant within ±1 unit for the whole settle (120 ticks = 2 s = 5 × `LOOKAHEAD_TAU` of 0.40 s), and the beam does not creep after the mouse stops. While the avatar *walks* with a still cursor, `f_q` follows the avatar at the follow lag: at a steady pace the aim is shifted by `S`: exactly constant (−34 px at the run) only at 60 fps with a constant `tick_alpha_draw` (0.05) and integer `pos_curr`, otherwise within `ceil(v / TICK_RATE)` = 3 px of constant (`S` ∈ {−35, −34, −33} at 90 and 144 fps in the pipeline D.14b fixtures); when a run starts or stops `S` changes by at most 36 px at `v_run` (24 at walk): the lag part ≤ 32.4 px (the dt→0 bound; 30.9 px at 60 fps), the tick phase ≤ 3 px, floor 1 px *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; and `aim_q` follows the formula (`(f_q − pos_curr) + cursor_off − (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)`), within ±1 unit of the computed value — the recorded PC-5 trade-off, a turn caused by the geometry of the camera's trailing pose and never by the look-ahead. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review, delegated ruling R4)* Diagnostics: `PC_AIM_DRIFT` (Adapter — ticks where `aim_q` changed by ≥ 2 units with the cursor and the avatar both still; expected 0; the expected 0 holds only for a still avatar, or a run at constant `tick_alpha_draw`: a steady run at 90 / 144 fps may change `aim_q` by the 3 px phase step, ≈ `atan(3/60)` ≈ 2.9° at a 60 px cursor, which is not drift *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*).
- **If the cursor is over a HUD element**: in `gameplay` the cursor is confined to the window and the HUD captures no mouse input (the hand rail is display-only; HR-11 bracket navigation is key/pad); the cursor's aim point is still well-defined through the look-ahead-free mapping (`cursor_aim_world`, PC-5; not C11's `pose_q` mapping, which is for prompts) and aim continues to track it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4: the stale "through C11" is removed)*. Only a non-`gameplay` context releases the cursor to the UI. No diagnostics.
- **If the cursor is in the letterbox**: the aim mapping (`f_q + (cursor_vp − picture_centre) / k_eff`, window px over `k_z`) returns a world point outside the visible field; aim is direction-only, so `θ_raw` is still valid and the beam points toward it; no prompt can be there (nothing is drawn in the letterbox). The confinement rectangle is the window, not the field *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; a cursor that hits an invisible wall at the field edge would read as aim assist, and the letterbox is only 0–80 px at common displays)*. No diagnostics.
- **If the right stick is released**: `live` goes false → raw aim holds the last angle; `live_changed` forces one `set_aim {aim_angle: unchanged, live: false}` at the next interval slot (≤ 3 ticks), after which the sim's facing falls to rules 5–6 (movement octant while moving; holds when still); the drawn light holds its angle throughout. Re-deflecting past `AIM_DEADZONE` sends `live: true` the same way, with the new angle. With `aim_release_mode = FOLLOW_ALWAYS` the stick is never live and nothing is sent on release; with `FOLLOW_MOVE_WHEN_RELEASED` the Adapter then slews the held aim towards the movement octant at `AIM_FOLLOW_SLEW` and sends as D.7 allows. No diagnostics.
- **If the aim flicks exactly 180° in one frame**: `Δ = 2048` (the circular maximum) → one send, subject only to the 3-tick interval; facing crosses four sectors in one tick (the hysteresis compares distance from the *current* sector's centre, 2048 ≫ 320); the drawn light turns the same frame; the camera look-ahead slews at the pipeline's `L_SLEW_MAX`, which is the only thing that lags. No diagnostics.
- **If the aim source switches (mouse ↔ stick) during a channel**: `source_changed` forces a send bypassing the threshold; facing stays locked (rule 2 outranks aim); the chest lamp follows the new source from the locked posture's chest point; the channel is untouched. The glyph set follows the last *button* device independently (250 ms hysteresis), so a stick aim with keyboard verbs keeps keyboard glyphs. Diagnostics: `PC_AIM_SOURCE_SWITCH` (Adapter).

**Facing and animation**

- **If facing changes mid-walk-cycle**: the sheet (and possibly `flip_h`) changes on that tick; `anim_counter` is untouched, so frame 5 of the E walk becomes frame 5 of the NE walk — the stride is continuous and the feet do not re-plant (PC-4 "instant, mid-cycle"). The chest point moves to the new row's value (≤ 4 px on a mirror); the tool sprite swaps hands. No diagnostics.
- **If a mirror flip would occur while channelling**: it cannot — facing is locked to the target's octant for the whole channel; the sheet and `flip_h` were chosen at `begin_*` apply. If the *target* moves across the actor's axis (it cannot at MVP — targets are fixtures) the lock still holds the original octant. No diagnostics.
- **If the player back-pedals at full speed and then turns to face the direction of travel**: back-pedal is full `v_walk` (`BACKPEDAL_FACTOR = 1.0`); while `dot(UNIT[octant], UNIT[facing]) < 0` the cycle runs reversed (`dir = −1`); on the tick facing crosses to the travel side `dir` becomes +1 and `anim_counter` continues from its current value (D.11) — no frame jump, no re-plant. If the facing change also changes the sheet, the same frame index is drawn on the new sheet. Position and speed are unaffected throughout. No diagnostics.

**Focus, channels, carrying**

- **If a channel's target becomes unlit mid-channel**: `begin_*` requires `lit` at apply; thereafter the owner re-checks exposure on its own cadence (every 6 ticks via the same exposure query the prompt uses) and **aborts** if the target is unlit at a check — the "interrupted" cue, bar snaps empty *(**adopted by Interaction** — IA-7 and IA-14: the check cadence is `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` = 6 and a dark gap of ≤ 5 ticks is tolerated; the rule is no longer a recommendation. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Proposed grace, Interaction's to adopt (Open Question 25):** `LIT_ABORT_GRACE_TICKS` (30, accessibility-extendable) counted in consecutive unlit ticks, so tremor, stick drift or sliding off a narrow beam edge cannot abort a 360-tick weld on one unlit check while a switched-off lamp still aborts inside half a second. Rationale: "what you light, you can touch" must hold for the duration or a 4–6 s cut becomes a free Attention dodge — light the hatch, go dark, finish in the black. Cost: toggling your own beam mid-work aborts your own work, which is honest. The 6-tick cadence tolerates a single-tick flicker.)* Diagnostics: `PC_CHANNEL_ABORT{unlit}` (owner's channel, mirrored into the controller's abort counter).
- **If a movement intent applies on the same tick the channel would complete**: channel owners declare their progress hooks `after: controller.step` so they read **this** tick's `octant`; each run evaluates **abort before accrual** (octant ≠ NONE → abort; else accrue; then complete if ≥ duration), so the move wins and the channel aborts one tick short — the stillness requirement holds on every accrual tick including the last. A `cancel_channel` in P2 of the completion tick likewise prevents completion (the ref is gone before P3). The window is one tick (16.7 ms); the player sees the bar drain from full. *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; PC-15 does not place the owners' hooks relative to `controller.step`; without `after:` an owner would read last tick's octant and a channel could complete one tick after the player moved. Interaction/Assembly to confirm.)* Diagnostics: `PC_CHANNEL_ABORT{move}` and `PC_CHANNEL_ABORT_AT_COMPLETION` (sim — a count of the exact same-tick case, expected rare).
- **If two candidates have identical scores**: the tie breaks on smaller `θ_a`, then smaller entity id (D.9) — deterministic and stable across frames; with `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN = 0.10` the current focus keeps the prompt unless the challenger is better by 0.10, so two equidistant objects never alternate as the aim jitters. The Adapter's choice is presentation; the sim only validates the chosen target. Diagnostics: `PC_FOCUS_TIE` (Adapter), `PC_FOCUS_SWITCHES` per second.
