### Performance budget

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this block is **advisory**, lives in `tests/performance/controller/` and is owned by the **technical-director**; wall-clock timings are not deterministic and are removed from the `(auto)` suite, so a slow CI machine can never fail a logic test.)* Measured by the diagnostics timer on the reference machines **RM-DESKTOP** (minimum-spec desktop: Windows 10/11 64-bit, i5-9400F / Ryzen 5 2600 class, 8 GB, GTX 1050 Ti / RX 570 4 GB class, 1920×1080 at 60 Hz) and **RM-DECK** (Steam Deck LCD, 1280×800, 30 fps target), on a release export (the Godot 4.7.2 release template, renderer `forward_plus`, vsync on, 120-frame warm-up, the renderer and driver read-back recorded with each result; `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`), with the Interaction, Emitter, Suit and Attention stubs in place. Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12 (user delegated all remaining calls, including naming the reference machine; supersedes R11): the figures below are per-component sub-budgets, pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK; they are not the frame-level P99 ceilings (16.6 ms / 33.3 ms with 90 % headroom), which are the technical-director's. The developer's machine is not a reference machine; figures from it are indicative only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* **Every figure below is this section's proposal, not a Section D value — the GDD gives no CPU budget for the Adapter, the Controller Sim, `LocomotionStep` or the Presenter.** A miss logs and is raised to the technical-director; it does not fail the suite until the motion-integrity spike has confirmed or replaced the figure.

- **AC-PCI-217** **GIVEN** the Adapter with keyboard, mouse and a pad all active, 32 candidate interactables in reach and a sweep in progress, **WHEN** 1,000 frames run, **THEN** sampling, quantisation (D.6, D.7), focus scoring (D.9) and submission together take ≤ 0.1 ms per frame at P99. [PC-1 (a); D.6, D.7, D.9]
- **AC-PCI-218** **GIVEN** `LocomotionStep` against a Layout fixture of 2,000 footprint rectangles with a spatial index, **WHEN** 1,000 ticks run for one actor, **THEN** the step takes ≤ 0.05 ms per actor per tick at P99; with 4 actors ≤ 0.2 ms. [PC-3, PC-14; D.3]
- **AC-PCI-219** **GIVEN** `controller.step` for one actor, **WHEN** 1,000 ticks run, **THEN** it takes ≤ 0.02 ms per actor per tick at P99 (facing, `v_eff`, state machine, chest point, noise push). [PC-15; D.4, D.8, D.12]
- **AC-PCI-220** **GIVEN** the Avatar Presenter for one body with the tool layer and three overlays, **WHEN** 1,000 frames run, **THEN** its per-frame write (interpolate, floor, frame index, `flip_h`, overlay visibility, light origin and rotation) takes ≤ 0.1 ms per avatar at P99; four bodies ≤ 0.4 ms. [PC-1 (c), PC-11; D.11, D.12]
- **AC-PCI-221** **GIVEN** a 10 s full-rate aim sweep plus octant changes every 10 ticks, **WHEN** submissions are counted, **THEN** the Adapter submits ≤ 26 commands per second (20 aim + 6 move) and never more than one of each intent type per tick. [PC-2 "far inside `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK`"; D.7]
- **AC-PCI-222** **GIVEN** the controller's `PC_*` diagnostics enabled, **WHEN** 1,000 ticks run, **THEN** the counters add ≤ 0.01 ms per tick (dev-only cost; absent in release except Ship Body's `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP`, which is Body's budget). [Edge Cases preamble]

### Gaps / untestable as written

- **Formulas D.13–D.16** are all authored and cited from E and G; the earlier gap about missing blocks is closed. *(Resolved 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*
- **Ship Body GDD is now written → collision fixtures still hand-placed.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Body's GDD exists and takes PC-14, the foot box and `trunc`; fixtures stay hand-placed until a Layout `PROJECTOR` build exists. The "Ship Body GDD unwritten" and "PC-14 provisional" wording below is superseded.)* Every `LocomotionStep` criterion (PC-18 tests 5–8, the door leaf, the seam detach, the door-close overlap) runs against a `collide` fixture of hand-placed integer rectangles, not the Layout `PROJECTOR`. The foot box (16 × 8), the 1/256 fixed point, `trunc` rounding and the `tests/unit/body/` location are all Body's to confirm (PC-14 is adopted; only the technical-director's ratification is pending); if Body changes the mechanism the tests move with it.
- **Interaction's reason classes.** Reason classes are declared by Interaction (IA-10, IA-12; Open Question 5 answered); the free-lie-detector case is an `Applied` channel resolving to `PRECONDITION` (KRN:96). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system)* The ack-identity and deferred-continuation criteria run against an Interaction stub that declares both classes; the `BUSY`, `OBSTRUCTED` and `OUT_OF_CHARGES` reasons, the `CLOSING_BLOCKED` state name, `anchor_o` defaults, drop placement, partial-progress persistence and the 6-tick unlit re-check are all stub assumptions until Interaction and Layout land.
- **Suit thresholds.** `locomotion_cap` is read from a Suit stub; when CRITICAL and FAILED map to `WALK_ONLY`/`NONE`, and the divisor-6 staleness, are Suit's (Open Question 9). The recovery-at-`death_tick` criterion assumes `suit.vitals` precedes `controller.step` in hook order, which PC-15 declares but Suit's registration must honour.
- **Motion-integrity spike items 12/13, plus the target-acquisition item** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the aim-acquisition protocol of Game Feel is added to the spike)*. The 144 Hz read of the 1,1,2 / eight-2s-then-3 cadences, the carry diagonal's 29 % zero-motion ticks, and the input-to-first-pixel latency are judged on a build; headless tests check only the step properties (no backward or 2-px step). The spike's verdict may change the cadence rules and thus the (auto) golden values.
- **Headless cannot capture frames.** `commands.test` has no rasteriser and no device: every pixel-level, audio and cursor-confinement criterion is `(manual)` with retained evidence; the `(auto)` set tests the quantisers on fed vectors, the Presenter's *outputs* (frame index, `flip_h`, position, light members) rather than pixels, and cue *ids* rather than sound. The ack-identity criterion in particular is proven on outputs headlessly and on pixels/audio only by capture. A windowed CI runner would promote roughly 12 criteria.
- **Identical-input-trace test needs two things that do not exist:** a recorded input-trace format for the presentation side and read-view fixtures for calm / tell / breach and sanity stages 0–3 (the Attention, Horror and Sanity GDDs are now written; no build supplies their read views yet). The criterion is written against stand-ins; the "stage 0–3" count is the render GDD's naming and the Filter GDD must confirm it.
- **`set_aim` under ±2° jitter never reaches zero sends.** D.7's settle clause fires whenever `0 < Δ` persists 6 ticks, so a jittering mouse sends ≈ once per 6 ticks (the Tuning Knobs row for `AIM_SETTLE_TICKS` admits "a slow mouse drift … defeats the threshold"). The criterion asserts the honest bound (≤ 10/s, every value within 23 units); if the designer wants zero sends under jitter, the settle clause needs a `Δ ≥ k` floor — a Section D change, not a test change.
- **Stale text the amendment superseded** — all four items ((a) pad default Edge Case, (b) channel interrupt modes, (c) Open Question 10 wording, (d) Dependencies invariant) were rewritten in place on 2026-10-26 by the consistency check; the Dependencies row states both invariants with I2 (< 200 px/s) binding.
- **`PC_*` diagnostics names are frozen.** Every criterion reading a counter (`PC_RESYNC{…}`, `PC_CHANNEL_ABORT{…}`, `PC_FOREIGN_ACTOR_REJECT`, …) uses the names listed in Edge Cases; this GDD freezes them under the Debug Overlay's channel convention (registry `diag_panel_contract`: the owner's prefix `PC_*`, units in the declaration, not the name); `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` is Ship Body's (registry). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values)*
- **Lamp-occlusion lint needs a mask that does not exist.** The technical-artist's per-sheet lamp-plate mask and the import hook are unspecified; the criterion is written against a fixture mask. Likewise the `CHEST_POINT`/`HAND_POINT` tables have no sheets yet (`/asset-spec` pending), so the D.12 golden values use the GDD's example numbers.
- **Cross-GDD lint collision: "no `Input` reads outside the Adapter".** As written, the lint fails against the render GDD's own criterion that scans "the pipeline root's input mapping" for `device == 0` and against C11 mouse-to-world, which needs the cursor position. Resolved by ADR-0019 § 6.4 and § 8.1: the Adapter owns every Input read, the pipeline root reads none, zoom edges reach it by `request_zoom_step` and the cursor is published as `AdapterViews.get_cursor_px()`; the lint carries an allow-list of the Adapter's registered files *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0019 sync)*. (Earlier text:) Either the Adapter hands the cursor position and zoom edges to the pipeline root, or the lint carries an allow-list naming the pipeline root. That is the pipeline owner's call; both GDDs' Section H should agree before the lint is written. The ten dev-tag `debug_overlay_*` actions (PC-2) join this collision on the Adapter's side: the Adapter is their only `Input` reader and forwards them host-locally, so the Overlay makes no `Input` read of its own. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values)*
- **`DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` semantics — resolved 2026-10-27.** PC-13 says "glyphs follow the last button/key device with 250 ms hysteresis" without saying whether 250 ms is a minimum dwell on the new device before switching or a minimum interval between switches. The criterion assumes the latter, with the pending device honoured when the interval expires; HUD & Suit Layer adopts that minimum-interval reading (K24), so the timestamps stand. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)*
- **End-to-end device input is manual.** gdUnit4 can feed synthetic `InputEvent`s but not a real pad's dead-zone behaviour, SDL3 merging or OS cursor confinement; the device-switch, mixed-input and confinement criteria are (auto) on fed events and (manual) on hardware.
- **Co-op criteria are seam-only.** `MIRROR` construction fails in MVP (kernel H8), so the remote-body, replication-quantum and foreign-seat criteria run on read-view fixtures and the kernel's test ingress; they are re-expressed against real sessions in VS-B. The `throw` binding (Q / RB) follows the ux-designer's ruling of the 2026-10-27 design review. **The gdUnit4 harness is not installed**: every `(auto)` criterion is blocked on the prerequisite story in the preamble of this section.
- **Performance figures are unsourced.** The GDD gives no CPU budget for this system; the block above proposes 0.1 / 0.05 / 0.02 / 0.1 ms and is provisional until the spike; a miss logs, it does not fail.
- **Two additions this section makes that the GDD does not state.** The test directories `tests/unit/body/` and `tests/unit/controller/` and the evidence directory `production/qa/evidence/controller/` are proposals (the kernel and pipeline sections used `tests/unit/kernel/` and `tests/unit/render/`); the directory-structure doc should confirm the siblings. The D.4 exhaustive-precedence criterion and the "sensitivity control" for the identical-trace test are this section's additions — accept or strike.

| 1 | **CLOSED against Ship Body & Interior Physics (PC-14).** Ship Body's GDD (designed) owns ship-local integer position, the fixed-point integration (`trunc`, residue reset on the first octant change, preserved across `post_restore`), collision sweeps, the foot box 16 × 8, body transfers and `odometer`; the Controller Sim owns intent, gait, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references. Only the technical-director's ratification in the weld spike remains *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; was "recommended / provisional")* | technical-director | Ratification only; before the weld spike |
| 2 | **RESOLVED — user decision 2026-10-26: the lamp is chest-mounted and hands-free (Pandorum).** The beam is available in every state the player can act in — carrying, channelling, running — because nothing the hands do touches it; carry frames keep the chest lamp unobstructed. Consequences: beam origin = per-facing chest point (not the hand point), mirrored symmetrically; the tool layer carries no light; `toggle_beam` remains the only beam input. **Throwable glowsticks** (Pandorum) are adopted as the deployable-light item: omnidirectional, thrown along a short arc and landing as a world light the web can see — owned by Suit & Equipment (item, charges) and Interaction (throw verb/arc); art-director to set their colour within the palette (deployables are currently specified as flickering Tungsten pools; a chemical-stick colour must not borrow a loop hue or the sanity hues) | art-director (3.2, 5.1, 5.5, 5.6, 8.3 amendments; carry-frame rule; glowstick look); Suit & Equipment; Interaction | Before `/asset-spec` for this system and the Suit GDD |
| 3 | **Walk 2 vs 1 px/tick.** If the MVP playtest reads the boarding rhythm as hurried, 1 px/tick (1.25 tiles/s) is the recorded alternative; it costs frame-repeat at 144 Hz (render D.17) and halves crossing speed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: with invariant 3 now strict, `v_walk = 1` needs `v_carry < 1`, which the whole-pixel rule forbids, so the alternative is a combined walk + carry redesign (for example carry as one pixel every other tick through a new fixed-point carry step), not a one-knob change; it is the last fallback in the feel protocol.)* | game-designer | MVP playtest |
| 4 | **RESOLVED — the inspect card ships live at MVP** (In-World Instruments IW-21); `inspect` is a presentation-only context switch (IA-38), the Adapter owns `inspect_card`, and `set_aim` stays live there (PC-16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)* | In-World Instruments GDD | Closed 2026-10-27 |
| 5 | **ANSWERED by `interaction-system.md` (IA-10, IA-12; KRN:96: the free-lie-detector case is `Applied` and resolves to `PRECONDITION`)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system)* — **Immediate vs deferred rejection classes** — Interaction declares which domain reasons are immediate and how a `PRECONDITION` on a perceived-broken device is charged before the reveal (the deferred-channel state the Adapter waits on, under the kernel's P4 cost rule) | Interaction GDD | Design order 10 |
